# Golden-set labeling worksheet (plan §12)

250 questions sampled from the Stage 4 canonical current Q&A pairs, stratified by year × product family (2025–2026 weighted for currency), with coverage guarantees for the FAQ families and evergreen topics. For each item, fill in the three labeled fields.

**Rubric** (per §12):

- **Points to hit** — 2–5 bullets a correct, complete answer must cover. Derive them from the source answer below; write them as verifiable statements, not topics.
- **Expected sources** — which authorities a correct answer should cite: authority 1 = products.json (approved claims, quote verbatim), 2 = PDSRG, 3 = QA canonical, 4 = podcast. Candidates are prefilled; check the ones that apply, add others.
- **Forbidden** — claim language the answer must NOT use (disease treatment/cure/prevention beyond approved copy, medication or condition guidance — anything on the §8 hard-escalation list must hand off to a human instead of being answered).

The **source answer** is the scrubbed expert answer the question was sampled from — the starting point for the points-to-hit, not the required answer text. Answers may be outdated on details; flag that in the points rather than copying it.

Splits alternate dev/test (125/125); labelers label both — the split only gates when the eval harness may look at them.

**Q:** Does dotFIT have any research on the attached 2008 New England Journal of Medicine study about alternatives to low-fat diets, such as Mediterranean diets?

- [ ] other: …

**Forbidden:**

- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> The bottom line:
>
> Calories count most in weight loss, but picking the foods that make up those calories that make you feel best is the key—pick the right ones for you personally and you will burn more calories (see attached “Calorie Is a Calorie” article).
>
> The studies attached give the latest take on diet makeup and weight loss. Basically, it all comes out the same long term, but the goal is to do something you can sustain because, long term, weight regain is generally seen and maybe more so for very high-protein dieters (see “Macro Comp” study attached).
>
> Faster weight loss occurs with higher protein, and protein is more satiating—at least in the short term.
>
> The Mediterranean diet is probably one of the healthiest since it contains good fats and carbs with lean proteins—and that’s why we have one for everyone in our program.
>
> Below are some related Q&A from our Fitness Vault (click on the links or visit the entire section of Fit-Pro Q&As):
>
> Is the high-protein/low-carbohydrate diet best? Why?
>
> What can we do to keep people motivated and break the weight loss and gain cycle permanently?
>
> 35. Why are so many people still so carb-cautious?
>
> Only because they don’t know the difference between good and bad carbs, and carbs overall are not as important, from an energy supply standpoint, for the average adult as they are for athletes or active exercisers. From a health standpoint, carbs such as fruits, vegetables, and whole grains are very important and should make up 40–60% of the diet, depending on your activity levels. Unfortunately, refined grains/foods and added sugars are also carbohydrates, generally less healthy and considered contributors to weight gain when it’s actually the calories causing the weight gain—they just happen to be supplied in lousy food choices. So the message to stay away from carbs is to stay away from the bad carbs, not the good, but the average person doesn’t hear that—it’s just easier to be carb-conscious all the way around.
>
> And finally, most people still don’t grasp the fact that total daily calories in and out are what count, NOT carbs!

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Physiology, including hormones, daily activities, and appetite, makes subtle changes post-menopause, often making life and weight control more difficult. But the equation for losing weight is the same: eat fewer calories than you burn. To increase, or at a minimum maintain, lean body mass (LBM) simultaneously requires proper dietary support—i.e., supplementation.
>
> Therefore, finding a diet she can stick with is the key, as well as maintaining the exercise program to complement the diet and supplementing properly so that you can feed muscle while starving body fat (see baseline supplement program below that I added AminoXXXL to help support lean body mass, especially in older adults). This protocol is what we call “favorably altering body composition,” and the goal you describe requires keeping or adding lean body mass (LBM) while losing body fat. The good news is that the dotFIT program will take you all the way. Once you create your trainer account, you can add your client and create their individualized program. I have cc [NAME] here to make sure you get set up properly with a trainer account. Then read below and feel free to forward this information to your client so they understand the process.
>
> Welcome to our family, good luck, stay tuned for [NAME], and we’ll be with you all the way.
>
> Building and/or Maintaining LBM While Losing Body Fat and the dotFIT Program
>
> Building or maintaining LBM while losing body fat, depending on age and training experience (younger and less experience make the gains easier and faster), is a formula that requires a few components that you need to adhere to:
>
> 1) A daily menu plan that has the correct calories and foods, including protein quantity matched to the body composition goal;
> 2) A dietary supplement program to feed muscle while starving fat—i.e., deliver high nutrition with little to no calories—meaning you will need at least a multivitamin and mineral (MVM) and extra protein to help recover and maintain structural and functional integrity (when calories are limited to reduce body fat, you also limit the nutrients that would otherwise be contained in those calories);
> 3) Regular unaccustomed resistance exercise.
>
> Note: Make sure you consume daily, between all food and protein shake sources, close to 1 gram of protein for every pound of lean body mass (LBM), or body weight if not overweight, divided 4–5 times daily, including using your protein shake before and after the workouts. See below.
>
> Fitness, Health, Activity Recovery, Body Fat Loss & LBM Support Program—Level 1 with AminoXXXL
>
> Daily:
>
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender https://www.dotfit.com/Womens-Multivitamin (or https://www.dotfit.com/Over50MV if over 50 years)
>
> Take as directed.
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> WheySmooth https://www.dotfit.com/WheySmooth-Chocolate, Vanilla or Unflavored* or other favorite dotFIT protein mix https://www.dotfit.com/store/nutrition
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 g of protein per pound of LBM, or weight if not overweight, daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> If exercising, use ~30–35 g 30–40 minutes before the workout and repeat the dose 30 minutes after the post-workout AX dose (see below).
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself, or mixed with desired supporting contents, to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> Super Calcium https://www.dotfit.com/super-calcium (read all food labels for calcium content, including dotFIT protein shakes)
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1,000–1,200 mg) from food or shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal. Males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> Super Omega 3 https://www.dotfit.com/superomega3
>
> Take 1 daily with a meal if not consuming 2–4 servings per week of fatty fish.
>
> Workout days
>
> AminoXXXL (AX) https://www.dotfit.com/aminoxxxl-lemonade, Blue Raspberry, watermelon, and Vegan Tropical Colada
>
> Take 1 scoop ~5–10 minutes before and may continue to drink during activity or workout.
>
> Take 1 scoop immediately following workout or activity.
>
> WheySmooth* use as directed to get:
>
> 30–35 g protein 30–40 minutes before workout and repeat the same dose ~30 minutes after the post-exercise AX dose.
>
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us, and we got you covered! No matter how much you work out or what exercises you perform, you won’t get rid of belly fat without a proper diet. You need to support your muscle (LBM) while losing body fat. Following the dotFIT program, including the supplement program below, will get rid of the belly fat, support LBM, and give you the energy you need while pursuing the goal because you will be feeding your muscle while starving body fat. Please read all below and choose one of the 3 options to get started—and reach out any time to one of our phone coaches (cc’d [NAME] here) for more information or help.
>
> Thanks again and good luck on your fitness journey. We will be with you all the way!
>
> Spot-reducing note
>
> Losing body fat anywhere on the body requires consuming fewer calories than you burn, regardless of the type of diet you consume. The goal is to find a diet that you’re comfortable with so you can stick with it until you reach your goal, knowing there is no such thing as “spot reducing”—i.e., fat leaves the body from wherever it’s genetically programmed to lose it, and sometimes the places you want it to leave from first do not happen until later—but it will happen if you stay in the calorie deficit. You do this by eating less, moving more, or a combination of the two until you reach the goal.
>
> Building or Maintaining Muscle While Losing Body Fat and the dotFIT Program
>
> Building or maintaining muscle while losing body fat, depending on age and training experience (younger and less experienced people find it easier and faster to make gains), is not often easy to accomplish because the formula requires a few components that you need to adhere to: 1) a daily menu plan that has the correct calories and foods, including protein quantity matched to the body-composition goal; 2) a dietary supplement program to feed muscle while starving fat—i.e., deliver high nutrition with little to no calories—meaning you will need at least a multivitamin and mineral (MVM) and extra protein to help recover and maintain structural and functional integrity (when calories are limited to reduce body fat, you also limit the nutrients that would otherwise be contained in those calories); 3) regular unaccustomed resistance exercise. Additionally, we placed the minimal supplement program we use for exercisers to healthily speed recovery and results while supporting health and fat loss.
>
> Note: Make sure you consume daily, between all food and protein shake sources, close to 1 gram of protein for every pound of lean body mass (LBM), or body weight if not overweight, divided 4–5 times daily, including using your protein shake before and after the workouts. See below. As you progress, you can add additional supplementation if you continue to want to lose more body fat and/or gain more muscle by regularly updating your program with your weight/body fat measurements (it will automatically update the diet and supplements), or use our supplement screener tool: https://www.dotfit.com/supplements-screener
>
> 3 options:
>
> You can visit one of our licensed health clubs using the club finder: http://www.dotfit.com/ClubFinder.aspx. The expert fitness professionals in our licensed health clubs can give you direct service for all your fitness goals, including nutrition needs (non-clinical).
>
> Alternatively, if a club visit is not a viable option, you can receive all your diet, supplement, and muscle-gain (and/or fat-loss) needs online from the dotFIT program. Go to the dotFIT home page and log in (using the icon in the far upper-right corner), and you will come to this page: http://www.dotfit.com/new-user-register. You may just click this link for now. From there, simply follow the instructions for creating your program and setting your goal. The dotFIT program will give you the meal-plan outline you can alter as desired, with the proper calories, carbohydrates, fats, and protein to get started, track intake, and monitor results. It will also give you your supplement recommendations (I placed one below also) and exactly how to use them. Every week when you enter your weight and/or body fat, the program will tell you what to do until you reach your goal, including recreating menus as necessary. Once you create the program and get menus, you can go to our recipe section on the dotFIT website to get recipes with meal-preparation instructions, including meal-replacement recipes: https://www.dotfit.com/learn/recipes. There is also a complete exercise program you can use to create the proper program to accomplish your goal—and again, just answer the simple questions, including entering your goal, and your exercise program will be created. However, if possible, we recommend one of our trainers to help you get started. On the dotFIT website, you’ll also see a category called “Learn,” where you will find many frequently asked questions and answers: https://www.dotfit.com/learn
>
> Reply again to this email or call us at [PHONE], and we will put you together with one of our phone coaches.
>
> General Health, Recovery, Weight Control/Body Fat Reduction/Toning, and Performance Supplement Program
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
>
> DotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 years or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 years if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 years use Kids; children 12–17 years use 1-Active.
>
> Take as directed with meals.
>
> WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix, based on caloric allotment for the goal.
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of LBM, or weight if not overweight, daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself, or mixed with desired supporting contents, to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> As needed:
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content, including dotFIT protein shakes)*
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (approximately 1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> Super Omega 3
>
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if more than 8 oz/week but less than 16; 2 if 8 oz or less); not necessary if consuming 16 oz or more per week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> *All food labels, including protein powders, must contain calcium levels by serving, so always check.
>
> Workout Days:
>
> WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix.
>
> Take 1 scoop approximately 30–40 minutes before the workout.
>
> Take 1 scoop immediately following the workout.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We do not sell individual iron supplements because supplementing iron beyond the normal small amount in a complete Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM) should always be determined by blood work. When you received your blood work, your doctor should have recommended an iron supplement. It sounds like the one you purchased was a form of iron that often causes constipation or stomach distress. There are many different forms of iron supplements. Iron bisglycinate made by Solgar, in 25 mg capsules, may be gentler for your stomach. But if not, consult your doctor again, and be mindful that only your future bloodwork can determine the proper dosage or cessation.
>
> Hope this helps and have a great weekend!

> “There appears to be a clotting risk from using erythritol,” Freeman said. “Obviously, more research is needed, but in an abundance of caution, it might make sense to limit erythritol in your diet for now.”
>
> In response to the study, the Calorie Control Council told CNN that “the results of this study are contrary to decades of scientific research showing reduced-calorie sweeteners like erythritol are safe, as evidenced by global regulatory permissions for their use in foods and beverages,” said [NAME], the council’s executive director. Therefore, the results “should not be extrapolated to the general population, as the participants in the intervention were already at increased risk for cardiovascular events.”

*thread_date: 2023-05-19 · topics: nutrient safety, upper intake limits, vitamin RDAs, vitamin and mineral benefits · source: `a689a2b5145d27eb` 2023/Vitamin RDAs, over RDAs, diagram, sweet spot, benefits diagram from article.docx*

- 1. …

- 4. …

**Expected sources:**

- [ ] QA `a689a2b5145d27eb` — 2023/Vitamin RDAs, over RDAs, diagram, sweet spot, benefits diagram from article.docx (authority 3)
- [ ] podcast segment (authority 4)
- [ ] other: …

**Forbidden:**

- disease-treatment/cure/prevention language beyond products.json wording (claims are quoted, never paraphrased); medication or managed-condition guidance (escalate per §8); dosage arithmetic the source answer does not do.
- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> I’m assuming you are referring to the RDAs? As you know, many RDAs for vitamins and minerals are considered too low, such as D, C, E, most B vitamins, etc., and most have a wide range of safety, and possibly more benefits, up to the Tolerable Upper Limit (UL). Therefore, somewhere between the RDAs and the UL is the sweet spot, like you have seen in all our lectures (see below). Call me if you need more information.
>
> Have a great weekend!
>
> Appendix 2 Summary Figure of Proposed VM Intakes
>
> Range for Potential Maximum Benefits (The Safe and Recommended Nutrient Range [SRNR])
>
> Readers are reminded that because of the synergy of essential nutrition throughout all phases of life, vitamins and essential minerals are dependent on each other in all areas of metabolism, and therefore insufficiencies of a single vitamin or mineral can cause a negative domino effect, albeit over time—i.e., insidious health risk effects.
>
> The SRNR is depicted below using the data from Table 2 and shows potential overall cellular functioning benefits at different VM concentrations ranging from deficiency to recommended. Typical known U.S. intakes from food alone are shown in the yellow area and, depending on the vitamin or mineral, reach about 40–85% benefits if the RDA is 100%. The “sweet spot” is shown in green, which, based on the DRIs, would be the ~RDA to slightly below the NOAEL and definitely below the UL. Maintaining this sweet spot should be a lifelong goal and entirely possible using a properly designed formula incorporating the criteria described in this article to fill in the known gaps, depicted as an average by the yellow/orange area.
>
> Definitions
>
> Nutrient (vitamins and essential minerals [VM]): a substance that provides nourishment essential for growth and the maintenance of life
>
> VM deficiency: gross under-consumed nutrient leading to overt symptoms that, left untreated (repletion), will cause eventual sickness or death
>
> VM insufficiency: nutrient intakes below the RDA or Estimated Average Requirement (EAR) that, left untreated (repletion), may not have overt symptoms, but insidious outcomes related to short- and long-term development and health, such as disease manifesting so gradually it would be established before becoming evident, thus likely irreversible.
>
> RDA: Recommended Dietary Allowance—the dietary intake level that is sufficient (not necessarily optimal) to meet the nutrient requirement of nearly all (97 to 98 percent) healthy individuals in a particular life stage and gender group.
>
> AI: Adequate Intake—a recommended intake value based on observed or experimentally determined approximations or estimates of nutrient intake by a group (or groups) of healthy people that are assumed to be adequate—used when an RDA cannot be determined.
>
> UL: Tolerable Upper Intake Level—the highest level of nutrient intake that is likely to pose no risk of adverse health effects for almost all individuals in the general population.
>
> NOAEL: No Observed Adverse Effect Level—the highest concentration or amount/dose of a substance found NOT to cause an adverse reaction.
>
> LOAEL: The Lowest Observed Adverse Effect Level—the lowest concentration or amount/dose of a substance found to cause an adverse reaction.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We stated your BMI is 26, which is generally considered slightly overweight (see chart below), unless you are heavily muscled. However, you are saying you are underweight; therefore, I placed a dietary support program below, along with suggested mealtimes that are common for adults in your age group to gain healthy weight. I have also cc’d [NAME] here to help clarify your needs, explain the program below, and alter it as necessary.
>
> Thanks again, and reach out to [NAME] as needed.
>
> BMI is used to broadly define different weight groups in adults 20 years old or older.
>
> Underweight: BMI is less than 18.5
> Normal weight: BMI is 18.5 to 24.9
> Overweight: BMI is 25 to 29.9
> Obese: BMI is 30 or more
>
> Performance/Size Plan & Instructions with Creatine Monohydrate in Baseline
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> ActiveMV - Multivitamin & Mineral Formula
> Take two (2) daily: one immediately following the first large meal; one after the final meal of the day.
>
> Super Omega 3
> Take two daily with a meal.
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content)
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000-1200 mgs) from food/shake intake. Using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with a typical diet, calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing.
>
> If needed, take one with a meal.
>
> FirstString
> Take two (2) scoops 30-45 minutes before a workout, or use a favorite dotFIT® bar for convenience.
>
> Take two (2) scoops 20-30 minutes after the post-workout AminoFormula dose.
>
> Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself, mixed with desired ingredients to add protein/calories as needed to daily requirements.
>
> Be sure to ingest approximately 1 gram (g) of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 4-5 times daily along with the pre/post shakes. The pre/post shakes combined generally supply 50-60 g of the daily protein requirement.
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy
> Mix one scoop (5 g) with 4-8 oz of a favorite fluid/shake and take four times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first five days. Thereafter, take one scoop once daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrates.
>
> Training days: Take one scoop before or after your workout with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> Non-training days*: Take one scoop with a meal or shake.
>
> *May substitute a favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> Meal Timings
> As possible, eat every four hours.
>
> Large pre-training/event meal: 2-3 hours before training.
>
> Large post-workout meal: approximately 30-60 minutes after the last post-workout supplement.
>
> Early morning training
> Eat a large pre-training-type meal the night before.
>
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training, as shown above, and follow the workout-day supplement schedule above.
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
> <1.5-hour break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink.
>
> 1.5-2.5 hours: small pre-training-type meal.
>
> >2.5 hours: normal pre-training meal.
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
> 16 oz 2 hours before activity (extra 8-16 oz 1 hour before on hot days).
>
> 4-8 oz every 20 minutes during activity.
>
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity.
>
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g., Gatorade) and water as directed.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. You can gain muscle weight using any protein powder if your total daily calories are conducive to muscle gain and your resistance training program is progressive (i.e., regularly unaccustomed). However, the most commonly used protein powder for muscle gain and performance is the FirstString since it is preset with the proper carbohydrate (CHO) to protein ratio for maximizing muscle hypertrophy. That said, if you are looking for an all-natural source, such as the ones you named (All Natural WheySmooth, BestPlantProtein), all you have to do is add whatever CHO you would like to make the same ratio as in FirstString (~2 CHO:1 P).
>
> See below for the recommended baseline dietary support program with mealtimes for building muscle that includes the proper use of your desired protein powder (just substitute the powder you prefer for FirstString as noted). After using this baseline supplement program, and you want to take your fitness goals further, you can use our supplement screening tool to get the proper recommendations. Reach out to one of our coaches at any time for more information or questions. [NAME] is cc’d here.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day!
>
> Baseline Health, Recovery, Muscle Gain and Performance Supplement Program
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video.
>
> Daily:
>
> - dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 years or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65 years if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 years use Kids; children 12–17 years use 1-Active. Vegans use 1-Vegan.
> - Take as directed with meals.
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> - FirstString (or favorite dotFIT protein such as All Natural WheySmooth or BestPlantProtein).
> - Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gram of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided five times daily.
> - Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
> - SuperCalcium. Read all food and shake labels. Using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with other typical diet calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing.
> - Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000–1200 mg) from food/shake intake.
> - Females take one or two daily with meals; if you need to take two, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal. Males take one only if necessary.
> - SuperOmega-3.
> - Take one or two daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take one if >8 oz/week but <16 oz; two if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days:
>
> - FirstString* (or favorite dotFIT protein such as All Natural WheySmooth or BestPlantProtein)—use as directed to get 25–35 g of protein 30–40 minutes before the workout and repeat the same dose immediately post-exercise.
> - *May substitute a favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> Meal timings:
>
> - As possible, eat every four hours.
> - Large pre-training/event meal 2–3 hours before training.
> - Large post-meal ~30–60 minutes after the last post-workout supplement.
>
> Early morning training:
>
> - Eat a large pre-training-type meal the night before.
> - Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training, as shown above, and follow the workout-day supplement schedule above.
>
> Tournament play (multiple games):
>
> - <1.5-hour break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink.
> - 1.5–2.5 hours: small pre-training-type meal.
> - >2.5 hours: normal pre-training meal.
>
> Fluid recommendations*:
>
> - 16 oz 2 hours before activity (extra 8–16 oz 1 hour before on hot days).
> - 4–8 oz every 20 minutes during activity.
> - 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity.
> - *Use electrolyte formula (e.g., Gatorade) and water as directed.

> All your fitness needs, including menu plans and achieving your body composition goal, are contained in the dotFIT program. If you have a trainer, you can both use it. >
> Or, you can simply use the LeanMR Meal Replacement Program, which is the most popular for weight/body fat loss and maintenance. As shown below, the complete program is the most successful, simple, and common dietary support and weight loss meal substitute/replacement program that you can incorporate into your daily meal plans. Following this simple program will help control appetite, ease your journey, and speed results while supporting health and lean body mass during weight loss and, most importantly, support weight maintenance. You can click here https://www.dotfit.com/MRweightlossplans for a simple, ready-to-go solution, including sample menus with proper calories, foods, and LeanMR meal replacement diet integration.

>
> Click on links for brief product descriptions, including a short video.

> Daily:
>

If 50 years or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females using competitive training protocols under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 years if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 years use Kids; children 12–17 years use 1-Active. Vegans use 1-Vegan.
>
> LeanMR or another favorite dotFIT protein mix: Use as directed within daily meal planning (see below) and to make sure you get approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of lean body mass, or weight if not overweight, daily from all sources divided 4–5 times daily. It can also be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself, or mixed with desired supporting contents, to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> As needed
>
> Super Calcium: Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium, approximately 1,000–1,200 mg, from food intake. Read all food labels for calcium content, including protein mixes. Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal. Males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> SuperOmega 3: Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz per week of fatty fish. Take 1 if consuming more than 8 oz per week but less than 16 oz; take 2 if consuming 8 oz or less. It is not necessary if consuming 16 oz or more per week unless for athletic recovery purposes; then increase as directed.
>
> Meal Replacement Integration
>
> Weight loss phase:
>
> Meal replacements generally supply two small meals a day and allow freedom of choice from traditional foods for the remaining allotted foods/calories. Therefore, use them as two small meals within any calorie-restricted meal plan of 4–5 meals, since it has been shown that frequent small meals are better for weight loss than fewer larger ones, especially as they relate to satiety, preservation of lean body mass (LBM), and energy levels.
>
> Get LeanMR recipes here: https://www.dotfit.com/category-cid-1868.html and menu plans here.
>
> Maintenance phase:
>
> Continue to consume 4–5 small meals daily within your caloric allotment that include 2 meal replacements for convenience and to help ensure overall diet quality while reducing food costs.
>
> You can go to our recipe section on the dotFIT website to get recipes with meal preparation instructions, including meal replacement recipes.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. If you have high blood pressure, you would not want to use ThermAccel since it contains stimulants. You can use Weight Loss & Liver Support (WLLS) if you are seeking assistance in bodyfat/weight reduction. WLLS helps to mobilize unhealthy liver fat that causes energy metabolism problems and does not contain stimulants. Below is a brief product summary and how it is commonly used in a baseline dietary support program that includes the menu integration of Meal Replacements/substitutes. Whatever you choose to use, make sure you clear it with your doctor.
>
> Thanks again and have a great week!
>
> WLLS Purpose
>
> Non-stimulant bodyfat loss aid to support liver health to improve overall metabolism, leading to proper energy substrate (dietary fats/CHO/proteins) usage/burning and enhanced appetite management, thus easing and accelerating the desired body composition journey.
>
> When the body regularly stores fat, some is deposited in organs such as the liver, compromising its overall functioning, especially the liver’s ability to manage the usage and burning of carbs/sugar and fat.
>
> WLLS’s natural ingredients, such as green tea extract, milk thistle, and choline, target mobilizing liver (belly) fat to improve sugar and overall fat metabolism/burning. WLLS also includes specialized antioxidants such as NAC and EGCG to reduce weight gain’s related oxidative stress—i.e., break the negative chain reaction of unhealthy weight gain.
>
> The synergistic and unique contribution of African Mango can improve lipid profiles and support appetite control to make the individualized fitness journey easier and faster.
>
> Especially important for very overweight or obese people (females >32% body fat and males >22%) to support liver health to begin a healthy bodyfat reduction journey.
>
> May be discontinued when the goal is achieved.
>
> Level-1 for Fast Results & Daily Recovery with Meal Replacement/Substitute Integration Program
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description including short video
>
> Daily:
>
> DotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender and follow label directions. If 50yrs or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use till 65yrs if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12yr use Kids; children 12-17yr use 1-Active. Vegans use 1-Vegan.
>
> Weight Loss & Liver Support
>
> Take 3 daily as directed: 1 before each of 3 meals or evenly split throughout the day.
>
> LeanMR (protein- and fiber-rich meal replacement formula)
>
> Use as directed within daily meal planning (see below) and to make sure you get ~1 gram of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, divided 4-5 times daily depending on preference.
>
> Can also be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired ingredients) to shore up total daily protein needs. See below for proper menu integration.
>
> Meal Replacement/Substitute Integration
>
> You may also click here for a simple ready-to-go solution including sample menus with proper calories, foods, and LeanMR meal replacement diet integration.
>
> Weight Loss Phase:
>
> Meal Replacements (MRs) generally supply two small meals a day and allow freedom of choice from traditional foods for the remaining allotted foods/calories. Therefore, use as two small meals within any calorie-restricted meal plan of 4-5 meals since frequent meals are often better for bodyfat/weight reduction than fewer larger ones, especially as it relates to satiety, preservation of lean body mass (LBM), and energy levels. LeanMR recipes are available here.
>
> Maintenance Phase
>
> Continue to consume 4-5 small meals daily within caloric allotment that includes 2 MRs for convenience and to help ensure overall diet quality while reducing food costs. A wide variety of healthy recipes, including meal replacement recipes, are available here.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The dotFIT Vitamin D supplement is purposely only Vitamin D3 so that people can add it to their dotFIT Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM)—all dotFIT MVMs contain 1,000–1,200 IUs [25–30 mcg] of Vitamin D—if and when they need more Vitamin D based on a blood test or time of year. The dotFIT MVMs all have between 80 and 100 mcg of Vitamin K, and you would not want much more than that daily from a supplement (unless for clinical reasons). Our SuperCalcium also has a small amount of Vitamin K (30 mcg/tab) and Vitamin D; in case someone is not using the MVM, the product will still be very effective. We make sure all products are synergistic to avoid someone crossing the Tolerable Upper Limit (UL) of any ingredient, so people will always be in a safe and effective range. See below for more information.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great weekend!
>
> More Info
>
> Make sure your total vitamin D intake from all supplement sources is as follows (see typical dotFIT user example).
>
> When blood tests are not available:
>
> Up to 70 years of age: consume 1,000–1,500 IUs per day of vitamin D3 from supplements.
>
> Overweight adolescents and adults, persons >70 years, active adolescents, and adult athletes: consume 2,000 IUs per day of vitamin D3 from supplements.
>
> When using blood tests:
>
> Up to 70 years of age: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL).
>
> >70 years of age: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 100 nmol/L (~40 ng/mL).
>
> Active adolescents and adult athletes: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 100–120 nmol/L (~40–50 ng/mL).
>
> The 70+ years and athletic recommendation for serum 25(OH)D concentration levels may likely require a minimum of 2,000 IUs per day (and often up to 4,000 IUs/d) of vitamin D3 supplementation and 1.5 times more in overweight individuals.
>
> Typical dotFIT user example reaching the serum 25(OH)D concentration of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL):
>
> 1,000–1,200 IUs of VDS from multivitamin and mineral formula (Active MV, Women's MV, or Over 50 MV).
>
> 0–400 IUs of VDS from calcium (Ca) supplement:
>
> 0 (if not needed), 200 IUs (500 mg of calcium if needed), or 400 IUs (if 1,000 mg/d of calcium is needed).
>
> 1,000 IUs from one (1) Vitamin D3 supplement.
>
> Total VDS will always be between 2,000 and 2,600 IUs, with this dosage being effective at reaching serum 25(OH)D concentration of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL).
>
> Typical Use dotFIT Vitamin D
>
> Note: 1.0 nmol/L = 0.4 ng/mL of 25-hydroxyvitamin D concentration (25(OH)D); 1 mcg D3 = 40 IUs.
>
> When using blood tests: anyone seeking or advised to increase their vitamin D status, as determined by their serum 25(OH)D concentration, to a level of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL) to maximize vitamin D's contribution to the body's structural development and tissue/organ maintenance throughout all phases of life. If below this standard level, start with one (1) soft gel daily and adjust as necessary.
>
> Up to 70 years of age: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 75–100 nmol/L (30–40 ng/mL).
>
> >70 years old: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 100 nmol/L (~40 ng/mL).
>
> Active adolescents and adult athletes: serum 25(OH)D concentration of 100–125 nmol/L (~40–50 ng/mL).
>
> Note: the >70-year and athletic recommendation for serum 25(OH)D concentration levels may likely require a minimum of 2,000 IUs/d (and often up to 4,000 IUs/d) of vitamin D3 supplementation and 1.5 times more in overweight individuals.
>
> When blood tests are not available (unless advised otherwise by a qualified health professional):
>
> If using other supplements (e.g., MVM, calcium, etc.) containing a total of 0–1,000 IUs (25 mcg) of vitamin D, take one (1) dotFIT Vitamin D3 soft gel with any meal.
>
> Up to 70 years: consume 1,000–1,500 IUs/d (25–37.5 mcg) of vitamin D3 from dotFIT supplements.
>
> Overweight adolescents and adults, persons >70 years, and active adolescents and adult athletes: consume 2,000 IUs/d (50 mcg) of vitamin D3 from supplements; therefore, take one (1) to two (2) dotFIT Vitamin D3 soft gels.

>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take one or two daily with meals; if you need to take two, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal. Males take one only if necessary.
>
> SuperOmega-3
>
> Take one or two daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take one if >8 oz/week but <16; two if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days
>
> FirstString* — use as directed to get:
>
> 25–35 gm protein 30–40 min before workout and repeat the same dose immediately post-exercise.
>
> *May substitute a favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> Meal Timings
>
> As possible, eat every four hours.
>
> Large pre-training/event meal two to three hours before training.
>
> Large post-meal approximately 30–60 minutes after the last post-workout supplement.
>
> Early morning training
>
> Eat a large pre-training-type meal the night before.
>
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training, as shown above, and follow the workout-day supplement schedule above.
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
>
> <1.5 hr break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink.
>
> 1.5–2.5 hr: small pre-training-type meal.
>
> >2.5 hr: normal pre-training meal.
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
>
> 16 oz two hours before activity (an extra 8–16 oz one hour before on hot days).
>
> 4–8 oz every 20 minutes during activity.
>
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity.
>
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g., Gatorade) and water as directed.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Beta-hydroxy-beta-methylbutyrate (HMB) is a metabolite of the essential amino acid leucine and fairly “old-school” as a dietary supplement for muscle building. The more modern version, ingredients contained in a product called AminoFormula (AF), far surpasses HMB’s potential to aid in recovery and support/build lean body mass (LBM). For these reasons, AF is commonly used throughout the sport and fitness industry to improve/maintain size and performance, and especially by older adults to help build muscle or stave off the inevitable. Further, it is recommended to all non-exercisers starting in the 4th decade of life to attenuate natural aging muscle loss.
>
> Below is a baseline health and fitness program that incorporates AF. In case you are interested in the deep science behind the product, click here, mindful that this document is contained in the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) used for certification and training purposes only. Below are links to user-friendly brief descriptions.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day!
>
> Baseline Dietary Support for All Sport & Fitness Goals
> (Click on product links for a brief product description, including short video)
>
> Daily:
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 yrs or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 yrs if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 yrs use Kids; children 12–17 yrs use 1-Active. Vegans use 1-Vegan.
> Take as directed with meals.
>
> Daily as needed:
> WheySmooth* or other favorite dotFIT protein mix.
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
> Super Omega 3.
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/wk fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/wk but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/wk unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days
> AminoFormula (AF)
> Take 1 to 1.5 scoops (as directed on label) ~5–10 min before and may continue to drink during activity/workout.
> Take 1 scoop immediately following workout/activity.

> Mix 1 scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first 5 days. >
> Creatine Monohydrate
> On training days, use one dose before workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> *If you are under 175 lbs, you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half—i.e., after the loading period, take a total of 5 g daily and split as described.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. You are comparing apples to oranges when comparing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (EC) with the Creatine HMB (CH) product you named. EC has beta-alanine (BA) and glutamine with the creatine, and CH has hydroxy methyl-butyrate (HMB), a downstream metabolite of leucine. Both contain 5 grams of creatine monohydrate (CM) per serving, but EC is a better performance product because of the added ergogenic effect of BA and glutamine for recovery. HMB can be effective in enhancing recovery at the proper doses. Unfortunately, CH is under-formulated at 1.5 grams per serving, and it only recommends one serving. The effective dose of HMB in studies is at least 3 grams per day. If you took the effective dose, the CH product at $49 would last you 15 days, but you would also get 10 grams of CM per day.
>
> If you are looking for added recovery along with the performance-enhancing effects of EC, add the AminoFormula (AF), which has far greater recovery powers than HMB and is why it is what all our pro, college, and serious recreational athletes use. See below for the EC and AminoFormula additions to a baseline supplement program for athletes.
>
> Note: If you are a dotFIT Practitioner, you get a significant discount on all products. If you are not, and you are a member of one of our licensed facilities, you may have a discount through them. Therefore, I’m connecting you with [NAME] to make sure you are set up properly.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day!
>
> ExtremeCreatineXXXL (ECr) Performance & Size Supplement Plan in Baseline (with AF—no protein stack)
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> ActiveMV — Multivitamin & Mineral Formula
> Take two (2) daily: one (1) immediately following the first large meal and one (1) after the final meal of the day.
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if consuming more than 8 oz/week but less than 16 oz; take 2 if consuming 8 oz or less); not necessary if consuming at least 16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> FirstString (or WheySmooth, depending on daily calorie allotment)
> Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself, mixed with desired ingredients, to add protein and calories as needed to meet daily requirements.
>
> Be sure to ingest approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 4–5 times daily along with the pre/post shakes. The pre/post shakes combined generally supply 50–80 grams of the daily requirement.
>
> AminoFormula (workout days only)
> Take 1–1.5 scoops, based on weight, approximately 10 minutes before the workout. You may continue to drink during the workout.
>
> Take one (1) scoop immediately following the workout.
>
> Begin ExtremeCreatineXXXL (ECr) at the second week.
> Take 2 scoops daily. Always take with some protein and/or carbohydrates, but within allotted calories based on body composition goal.
>
> On training days: take one (1) scoop before training with a pre-workout full meal, generally 2–3 hours before the workout. If not possible based on early training, take it with a pre-workout shake. Take the remaining dose, one scoop, anytime with a meal or shake to help evenly spread total creatine intake throughout the day.
>
> On non-training days: take one (1) scoop with the morning meal or shake and one (1) scoop with the evening meal or shake.

**Source answer:**

> Do you test for gluten?
>
> There is no gluten in the gluten-free products because there is no gluten substance in the product ingredients to start with, just like fruits and vegetables, fish, milk, etc., and therefore, they are not tested (imagine testing every food in the world for something that is not there in the first place). Mindful that in non-gluten-containing ingredients/products, there is no way gluten gets in unless there is a slim chance of cross-contamination because something is made/produced in a factory that also makes other products, as described in the email you just received. Please carefully read the entire email you just received and follow the directions/labels as necessary.
>
> Hope this helps.
>
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Although all our protein powders are gluten-free, if you have celiac disease, then you should not consume gluten-free products that are made in facilities that may use wheat in other products, which is why that information is on the label. Therefore, the two dotFIT protein powders you may use are All Natural WheySmooth and BestPlantProtein powder. I have cc’d [NAME] here to help you with an exchange, as necessary. I have also placed more information and a baseline dietary support program below that our Practitioners use for people with celiac disease that should be helpful.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great weekend!
>
> More information:
>
> None of the dotFIT products other than a few of the nutrition bars contain gluten. But if they are made in a facility that makes other products that contain gluten/wheat, then it must be put on the label for the people who may be highly allergic (such as in the case you mention, people with celiac disease), in case there would be a slim chance of some minute cross-contamination. Again, none of the dotFIT proteins or powders contain gluten. All our dietary supplements are also gluten-free, and many are made in facilities that are safe from cross-contamination (simply read the labels). Most of the nutrition food bars are not gluten-free. The peanut butter and jelly bar and the dotFIT Vegan bar are gluten-free. Click here for the gluten-free list in the store. And see below for more information on labeling. The labels on our products will always contain the proper information.
>
> FAQ
>
> Q: I'm a trainer and was wondering about the supplements that would be safe for our clients with peanut/wheat allergies or sensitivities (or worried about cross-contamination)? I'm trying to keep everyone safe while getting them the best supplements they can use.
>
> A: Please read every product label carefully, as all ours contain the proper warnings (see below), including gluten, shellfish, peanut, etc., for products that are made in facilities that may also make products with peanut/wheat extracts, such as one of our many powder manufacturers. Most of our pills/capsules, such as fish oil, MVMs, calcium, joint and skin support, etc., are made in facilities free of potential peanut or gluten cross-contamination and are therefore safe for your recommendations/use; warnings are not necessary. The last thing we want is for your clients to have a reaction, so please read all labels of products they are interested in for their fitness goals. Both our BestPlantProtein and All Natural WheySmooth can be used by your client as their protein supplement for their fitness goals since they are made in the proper facilities. Therefore, I put a simple baseline supplement program below that you can use with your client. You can then add as needed, making sure you read all labels, as this will be published on all labels only as needed:
>
> “Allergen Warning: This product was produced in a facility that may also process ingredients containing milk, eggs, fish, shellfish, tree nuts, peanuts, wheat, and soybeans.
> Contains: Milk, Egg, and Soy (from soy lecithin)”
>
> Note on AminoFormula:
>
> The Lemonade and Blue Raspberry AminoFormula are now produced in a facility that is free of shellfish, fish, shellfish, tree nuts, peanuts, and wheat/gluten. This is a recent facility change, so some old labels with the original warning may still be on the product. Label printings going forward will contain only an allergen warning of: “Contains: Milk, Soy (from soy lecithin) and Egg.”
>
> General Sport Health, Activity Recovery & Performance Support Program—Level 1 (Baseline—with nothing made in facilities that may use wheat in other products)
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
>
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender (if 50 years or older, use Over50; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females under 50 use 2-Active; children under 12 years use Kids; children 12–17 years use 1-Active). Click on videos in the store under “Overview” for brief descriptions.
>
> Take as directed.
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> All Natural WheySmooth (or you could also use the BestPlantProtein powder)*
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided five times daily.
>
> If exercising, use approximately 25 grams 30–40 minutes before a workout and repeat the dose immediately post-workout (see below).
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content, including dotFIT protein shakes)
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (approximately 1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take one or two daily with meals; if you need to take two, take one with the morning meal and the second with the evening meal. Males take one only if necessary.
>
> Super Omega 3
>
> Take one to two daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take one if over 8 oz/week but under 16 oz; two if ≤8 oz).
>
> Workout days
>
> All Natural WheySmooth or BestPlantProtein)*
>
> Take one scoop 30–40 minutes before a workout and repeat the same dose immediately after.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Although the dotFIT products will all test GMO-free, we do not pay for the extra testing certification (unnecessary added cost to consumers), which is why you don’t see it on labels (except for the Alln1 SuperBlendTM, which is automatically certified). All our testing and certifications are on branded ingredients and 3rd- and 4th-party testing, which assures purity, potency, safety, and efficacy. These certifications are expensive but in high demand for our Practitioner channel, including pro, college, Olympic sports, and our licensed health and fitness facilities.
>
> dotFIT products are Practitioner Products and therefore meet far different standards than products sold in consumer channels (please see below for details on Practitioner Product status).
>
> Have a great day!
>
> 3rd-Party Tested & NSF Certified for Sport Dietary Supplements
> dotFIT is Evidence Based* 24/7 Support with Practitioner Products Only
>
> dotFIT Worldwide is an R&D company that delivers holistic evidence-based programming, products, and support to over 1,500 sport, fitness, and health facilities, and over 40,000 sport and fitness professionals.
>
> #1 Recommended and Used Dietary Supplements in the Sport & Fitness Channels, including college and professional sports

>
> Q: Right now I'm taking LeanMR, and I noticed you have a pre/post protein powder. Which one is better for a workout and lose weight? Thanks
> A: The LeanMR is a specialized structured meal replacement (MR) that you would generally not use for a pre- and post-workout supplement because of the high fiber. The Pre/Post Workout and Meal Replacement Formula (PPWMR) can be used both as an MR and as your pre/post-workout supplement (also see recipe section). Mindful that the great part of protein powders is that you can add desired extra nutrition to get the calories, fiber, and macros that you want. The LeanMR starting formula has 6 g of fiber while the PPWMR has virtually none, but you can add as desired; therefore, it can be used for both purposes. If you choose to use the LeanMR for managing body fat/weight, then you would use the AminoFormula for your pre/post-workout supplement. **Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The quick answer is yes, you can add your CreatineMonohydrate (CM) to your NO7Rage drink. But since NO7 has 5 g of creatine monohydrate in the typical 2-scoop dose, we would rather have you take your separate CM at another time, such as after your workout with your post-workout shake or meal, to maximize daily creatine levels. See below for a complete program that is common to professional strength and performance athletes where both products are used. Ignore the loading phase since you have already been using the product and follow the maintenance instructions. Because you are 190 lbs, you will use 10 g of creatine daily between both sources.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great weekend.
>
> Adult Performance & Size Supplement Plan with Creatine Monohydrate & NO7 Rage (maintain BA levels)
>
> Active Multivitamin Mineral Formula
> Take two (2) daily: one immediately following the first large meal and one following the final meal of the day.
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16 oz; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> FirstString (or WheySmooth depending on daily calorie allotment)
> Take two (2) scoops 30–45 minutes before your workout, or use a favorite dotFIT® bar for convenience.
> Take two (2) scoops 20–30 minutes after the post-workout AminoFormula dose.
> Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself, mixed with desired ingredients, to add protein/calories as needed to meet daily needs.
> Be sure to ingest ~1 gram of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including food. Ideally, divide protein into 4–5 meals/day along with pre/post shakes (the pre/post combined generally supply 50–80 grams of the daily protein goal).
>
> AminoFormula (workout days only)
> Take 1–1.5 scoops (based on weight) approximately 10 minutes before your workout (add to NO7Rage if using this product). You may continue to drink during your workout.
> Take one (1) scoop immediately following your workout.
>
> Begin Creatine Monohydrate loading at the second week of training.
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy (if you are under 175 lbs, you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half*—i.e., after the loading period, take a total of 5 g daily and split as described below)
> Mix 1 scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of your favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first 5 days. Thereafter, take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
> Training days: take 1 dose before your workout and 1 after with meals/drinks (after the loading phase, you may discontinue the pre-workout dose because NO7 will supply Cr). May mix with pre/post-training formula.
> Non-training days*: take 1 dose with the AM meal and 1 dose with the PM meal.
> *Program total daily creatine for the first 5 days: 20 g/day. Maintenance phase: all days, 5–10 g. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day with some protein- and/or carb-containing meals/shakes.
>
> Dosing by body weight:
> Loading phase: 0.14 g/lb/day split into four (4) daily intakes of 5 g each, followed by a maintenance-phase dose of 0.04 g/lb/day for the duration of the supplementation period.
>
> NO7Rage3
> Daily (to maintain BA & Cr levels), take 1.5 to 2.5 scoops (depending on body weight). If necessary, because of caffeine sensitivity, start with a lower dose and increase to the proper body-weight dosage if not uncomfortably affected by caffeine. Take approximately 10 minutes before your workout (may mix with AminoFormula and continue to consume during your workout). See directions on the label for weight dosages.
> NO7Rage contains 150 mg of caffeine per scoop. As a reference: Starbucks Grande-drip coffee contains ~330 mg of caffeine.
> Non-training days, take 1 serving anytime of the day but do not use within 4 hours of sleep.
> *Program total daily creatine for the first 5 days: 20 g/day (plus 3.7–6.5 g from NO7 on training days). Maintenance phase: workout days = 7.5–12.5 g; non-workout days = 7.5–12.5 g. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day and around the workout as described and ingested with some carb and/or protein-containing meals or shakes. BA total daily intake = 3–5 g.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Yes, we do, but only if it is a blossoming athlete going to get to the next level, consuming the proper diet, and we have the parent’s permission. The products you name are perfectly safe and healthful for a 17-year-old normal healthy human, but from a business perspective we don’t promote them to minors. In other words, there is no scientific reason for not using these products in youth athletes, but there is the “business CYA” side. Therefore, it is up to the parents to approve when they have all the data, which you can supply through your Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) in your trainer console. I placed an FAQ below with more information if necessary. Further, the products you name have all been used safely for years with minors for various clinical reasons.
>
> Hope this helps. Have a great weekend!
>
> FAQ
>
> Q: Can youth athletes use creatine?
> A: Proper creatine supplementation is probably safe for most healthy adolescents, but our position is to not make a recommendation until the youth elite athlete is through puberty* and then with a parent’s consent once they have consulted with a qualified professional. Below is more information on position statements by other experts. I have also placed a baseline dietary support program below that we give all youth athletes and growing and developing children ages 5–17 years.
>
> *Puberty usually occurs in girls between the ages of 10 and 14; boys 12 and 16.
>
> Creatine supplementation in youth and adolescence
>
> General rule is ≥18 years unless a younger elite athlete is properly supervised with parent consent.
>
> The 18 years or older label cautions on creatine products have to do with manufacturers shielding themselves from liabilities and are not based on safety reasons, since science has documented CS safety in all age groups as shown and discussed above. 48, 49, 50, [i] The latest position by the International Society of Sports Nutrition (ISSN) is the following: “If proper precautions and supervision are provided, creatine monohydrate supplementation in children and adolescent athletes is acceptable and may provide a nutritional alternative with a favorable safety profile to potentially dangerous anabolic androgenic drugs. However, we recommend that creatine supplementation only be considered for use by younger athletes who: a.) are involved in serious/competitive supervised training; b.) are consuming a well-balanced and performance-enhancing diet; c.) are knowledgeable about appropriate use of creatine; and d.) do not exceed recommended dosages”.15, 28 i
>
> If you are further interested in youth and creatine supplementation, you are referred to the [NAME] et al. review, “Safety of CS in Active Adolescents and Youth,” Ref3.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We are assuming your body composition goal is to gain muscle since you entered that category in the subject line. Therefore, considering all your questions, it would be best to start with a baseline dietary support program below that includes your pre- and post-workout muscle gain protein formula and the Women’s Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM), which you can switch out for a prenatal MVM formula when you are ready. After using this baseline supplement program, if you want to take your fitness goals further, you can use our supplement screening tool to get the proper recommendations. Reach out to one of our coaches at any time for more information or questions. [NAME] is cc’d here.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day!
>
> Baseline Health, Recovery, Muscle Gain and Performance Supplement Program
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
>
>  dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. You will use the Women’s MV.
>  Take as directed with a meal.
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> FirstString
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided five times daily.
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> SuperCalcium (read all food and shake labels) (using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with other typical diet calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing).
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000–1200 mg) from food/shake intake.
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days
>
> FirstString* — use as directed to get:
> 25–35 gm protein 30–40 min before the workout and repeat the same dose immediately post-exercise.
> *May substitute a favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> Meal Timings
>
> As possible, eat every 4 hours.
> Large pre-training/event meal 2–3 hours before training.
> Large post-meal ~30–60 min after the last post-workout supplement.
>
> Early morning training
>
> Eat a large pre-training-type meal the night before.
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) and follow the workout-day supplement schedule above.
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
>
> <1.5-hour break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink.
> 1.5–2.5 hours: small pre-training-type meal.
> >2.5 hours: normal pre-training meal.
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
>
> 16 oz 2 hours before activity (extra 8–16 oz 1 hour before on hot days).
> 4–8 oz every 20 minutes during activity.
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity.
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g., Gatorade) and water as directed.

> Question: Do you take all the supplements on Lean 90 all together?
>
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. You can take the supplements in the Lean Pack 90 as directed over the 90-day period, meaning each supplement for 30 days, or you can take them all together. See below for more information.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> Q: Can I take all Lean Pack 90 products daily or individually over the 90 days?
> A: You can use the products as they are designed for the kit, meaning using Weight Loss and Liver Support the first 30 days; CarbRepel the second 30 days; and ending with ThermAccel for the last 30 days, for a total of 90 days. Or you can use them simultaneously if you need extra assistance or have a tight timeline to lose weight, since they all work by different mechanisms. Simply use each one as directed daily.
>
> Q: Do you recommend CR (or WLLS) and ThermAccel used together if I want to boost weight/fat loss?
> A: Yes, you can use CarbRepel with ThermAccel (TA), or any of the dotFIT weight loss products, such as Weight Loss and Liver Support, together. This is commonly done with people needing extra help, as each product works to support appetite and metabolism through different and unique mechanisms. Simply follow the directions on each product label. The links will take you to a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Yes, you can take Weight Loss and Liver Support and ThermAccel together. Just follow the individual directions. See below for related FAQs.
>
> Thanks again and have a great evening.
>
> FAQ
> Q: Do you recommend CR (or WLLS) and ThermAccel used together if I want to boost weight/fat loss?
> A: Yes, you can use CarbRepel with ThermAccel (TA), or any of the dotFIT weight loss products, such as Weight Loss and Liver Support, together. This is commonly done with people needing extra help, as each product works to support appetite and metabolism through different and unique mechanisms. Simply follow the directions on each product label. The links will take you to a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. You can take the three products any way you like, such as over the 90 days as the directions state, or you can take them all as needed each day as directed. See below for related FAQs.
>
> Thanks again and have a great week!
>
> Q: Do you recommend CR (or WLLS) and ThermAccel used together if I want to boost weight/fat loss?
> A: Yes, you can use CarbRepel with ThermAccel (TA), or any of the dotFIT weight loss products, such as Weight Loss and Liver Support, together. This is commonly done with people needing extra help, as each product works to support appetite and metabolism through different and unique mechanisms. Simply follow the directions on each product label. The links will take you to a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Q: Can I take all Lean Pack 90 products daily or individually over the 90 days?
> A: You can use the products as they are designed for the kit, meaning using Weight Loss and Liver Support the first 30 days; CarbRepel the second 30 days; and ending with ThermAccel for the last 30 days, for a total of 90 days. Or you can use them simultaneously if you need extra assistance or have a tight timeline to lose weight, since they all work by different mechanisms. Simply use each one as directed daily.
>
> Q: A client asked me if she should still take CarbRepel before a meal if not eating carbs at that particular meal. Also, what if you forget to take it 30 minutes before a meal? Can you take it right before, or should you wait until later?
> A: No, you would not use CR if not consuming carbs, or very little carbs, such as only vegetables or a little fruit. Taking CR as it was used in the clinical trials is ideal, approximately 30 minutes before a carb-containing meal, but it will still work if you take it at, before, or very close to the meal.
>
> Q: A client asked me if she should still take WLLS in the morning if she's not going to eat breakfast. Also, what if you forget to take it 30 minutes before a meal? Can you take it right before, or should you wait until later?
> A: The bottom line is that Weight Loss & Liver Support should be consumed three times daily and separated as evenly as possible to help maintain the desired levels of ingredients. Taking it before a meal is one way of spreading it out and getting help with appetite support within 30 minutes. But it’s not necessary once you start using the product regularly.
>
> And yes, have your client take it in the morning even if not consuming breakfast.
>
> More Info:
> Original Lean Pack 90 purpose
> Complete program for non-program users
> Menus, exercise, supplements, and self-promoting with brand connection
> Rationale for cycling
> Marketing: psychological timeline, financially palatable, and full commitment
> Efficacy: 1) Liver support first when at heaviest; 2) different appetite support/targets to overcome adaptation; 3) most aggressive product (ThermAccel) at final phase, as natural plateaus are more common
>
> Current use
> Same as above
> May use as directed or all together
> Use each product as needed: TA daily, WLLS daily, CR with higher-CHO meals
> Common, easy, and complete product to sell at challenges

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Yes, your product is still safe and effective well past the expiration date. See below for more information.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> More Info
>
> The “Expiration Dates” are basically a “best sold by date” with the product still potent. All dates are 2 years after production, only because you don’t need much more than that based on inventory turnover. But the product is still fine (safe and effective) many months to at least 2 years after the date in case you have not used it in a while, and kept it sealed.
>
> The definition of an expiration date for nutritional products such as ours is that the product must be within 10% of label claim of each ingredient on the last day, meaning it’s still at declared potency 2 years following production (only because they were tested at that milestone and all is still perfect, and generally no one carries more than a few months of inventory and the next lot has a new date that starts all over). Therefore, our pills and powders have a 2-year expiration and are still safe and effective to use well after the date because the only issue might be that they are a little less potent than when manufactured, but that is something that would only happen well after the published date. In the case of expired nutrition bars, the product is still safe and effective after the date but not as fresh tasting, and therefore we recommend you replace it with a new lot because taste matters.
>
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Yes, it is safe and still effective long after the expiration date. Probably at least another 2 years before there is any significant loss of potency on most products. If we retest them at the expiration date, we can always add another 2 years. We just never have to because we sell out long before the date. But people may have older non-used jugs at home, or on a shelf, and as long as it was sealed so nothing strange entered it, it will be fine. See below for more on expiration dates.
>
> Have a great New Year!!
>

> Typical Use
>
> Non-stimulant body fat-loss aid
>
> Recommended for overweight people to support a complete weight loss program, including related liver health.
>
> Take one tablet three times daily, generally 30 minutes before meals with at least 8 fl. oz. of water, or spread three tablets evenly throughout your awake hours.*

> *The bottom line is that WLLS should be consumed three times daily and separated as evenly as possible to help maintain the desired levels of ingredients, regardless of total daily meals or frequency. >
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The bottom line with Weight Loss & Liver Support (WLLS) is that one dose (pill) needs to be taken three times daily, spread as evenly as possible throughout the day, and this will maintain the ingredients’ effectiveness. Taking it before meals is more of a reminder and probably slightly better for the African Mango appetite support. Again, just make sure to take one dose three times daily, spread as evenly as possible throughout your awake hours, and you will be fine. See below for a related FAQ.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day!
>
> Q: A client asked me if she should still take WLLS in the morning if she's not going to eat breakfast. Also, what if you forget to take it 30 minutes before a meal? Can you take it right before, or should you wait until later?
>
> A: The bottom line is that Weight Loss & Liver Support should be consumed three times daily and separated as evenly as possible to help maintain the desired levels of ingredients. Taking it before a meal is one way of spreading it out and getting help with appetite support within 30 minutes. But it’s not necessary once you start using the product three times daily regularly, as proper blood levels will be maintained. And yes, have your client take it in the morning even if not consuming breakfast.

**Forbidden:**

- disease-treatment/cure/prevention language beyond products.json wording (claims are quoted, never paraphrased); medication or managed-condition guidance (escalate per §8); dosage arithmetic the source answer does not do.
- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Everyone in the supplement business is trying to tie a little science to something that naturally occurs in the body, suggesting that when added it can produce the same results as something big like GLP-1 drugs. Unfortunately, that is not happening at this point in time (when it does, we will be the first to know). See below for how someone would connect the AMPK protein/enzyme to weight loss, mindful that GLP-1 drugs suppress appetite, leading to voluntary weight loss. AMPK is activated when cellular energy is low to help mobilize glucose, but adding exogenous sources will do little, if anything, to shut down the appetite compared to the drugs.
>
> Have a great Memorial Day weekend!
>
> AMP-Activated Protein Kinase (AMPK)
>
> AMP-activated protein kinase plays a role in cellular energy homeostasis, mostly to activate glucose and fatty acid uptake and oxidation when cellular energy is low. The net effect of AMPK activation is stimulation of hepatic fatty acid oxidation and ketogenesis; stimulation of skeletal muscle fatty acid oxidation and glucose uptake; inhibition of cholesterol synthesis, lipogenesis, and triglyceride synthesis; inhibition of adipocyte lipogenesis; inhibition of adipocyte lipolysis; and modulation of insulin secretion by pancreatic β-cells.
