- disease-treatment/cure/prevention language beyond products.json wording (claims are quoted, never paraphrased); medication or managed-condition guidance (escalate per §8); dosage arithmetic the source answer does not do.
- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us, and congratulations on the progress. It is very simple using the dotFIT program to stay on track. When you enter your weekly weight or biweekly body-fat measurement, the program automatically tells you what to do. Or you can stay ahead of the response by going back to your goal page and entering your new body weight and/or body fat and goal. The program will alter your daily calorie intake appropriately. I placed more information below that gives you an idea of how everything works so that you would never come to a plateau until you want to—that is, until you reach your body-composition goal.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great Sunday.
>
> More information:
>
> This will give you the information you need to handle any plateau problem. It is how the dotFIT program works to continue losing body fat as you measure the client on weekly or biweekly measurement days.
>
> Diet Monitoring & Adjustments for Body-Fat Reduction
>
> Program Algorithms
>
> Adjustments:
>
> Measurable or visual reduction in body fat and/or weight proceeds in a consistent manner, e.g., a decrease in circumference inches and/or the desired average decrease in body fat per week.
>
> If progress stops or slows dramatically, use one or a combination of adjustments to restart the process:
>
> - Increase daily activities, such as daily steps or other non-athletic/exercise activities.
> - Standing and pacing burns 1.5–2 times more calories than sitting for the same period.
> - There are approximately 2,000–2,500 steps, depending on stride length, in a mile. Walking 2,000 steps will burn approximately 75–150 more calories, depending on individual size, than sitting for the same time; it takes approximately 20–30 minutes and can be done anywhere, even in the office, while on the phone, or watching TV.
> - Increase cardio workout time and/or intensity, and include interval training if necessary.
> - Example: If doing 30 minutes 5 days per week, go to 45 minutes and add interval work within the time frame.
> - Decrease food intake by approximately 200–300 calories per day, or remove a small portion of carbohydrates or fats from the largest meal other than the pre-workout meal. Incorporate 3 days low/1 high as necessary.
> - Add dietary support beyond baseline as necessary, such as thermogenic, energy partitioning, or appetite control support.
>
> Repeat the process any time body fat is stable for at least one week. Always remember, if you are supplementing properly and you stop losing fat, you need to eat less, move/burn more, or use a combination of the two, regardless of what you read or hear from others. As you lower calorie intake, make sure your protein intake stays at or above 1 gram per pound of body weight to protect LBM.

**Source answer:**

> No reason to panic. This is a newshound trying to make news. First of all, Gatorade should only be used by athletes that train intensely in workouts/practices/games longer than 60 minutes because it contains everything your body is using/sweating (e.g., electrolytes including sodium, glucose, etc.). In other words, there is no reason that anyone in the general population needs to consume it, because they don’t need the glucose/sugar, calories, or sodium, etc. This fact exposes this writer’s ignorance of science because she did not state that first. Second, in all our vast experience with busted athletes, they always try to blame their cheating ways on something they ingested. They always say, “It must have been contaminated with the banned substance I got busted for.” Third, NSF doesn’t test gummies, and no one should ever take nutrients, including vitamins, minerals, etc., in a gummy form, because the ingredients cannot remain stable for any length of time in this type of matrix.
>
> In summary, ignore the article, but there is no reason for you to be using Gatorade unless you are intensely training for over 90 minutes in a warm environment, or you need to replace electrolytes and glucose during a bout with the flu.
>
> Elvis has left the building.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Always do your best (without pressure) to get your child to eat healthy. But we all know kids are picky eaters, and no diet will supply all the nutrition they need to grow and develop to their full potential. Therefore, just as we did while the child was in the womb, we continue to fill their essential nutrition gaps to maximize growth and development, including height. For your 5- and 7-year-olds, a perfect daily Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM), using a great-tasting protein/carbohydrate mix like FirstString to function as a delivery system for things kids don’t necessarily like but need (i.e., using a protein-based smoothie to include fruits, milk, veggies, etc.). It’s a win/win.
>
> See below and keep in mind all programs we create not only help prevent injury, speed recovery, and improve training/exercise-induced results, but also contribute to better overall health while maximizing growth and development potential, including height. Proper nutrition makes our children and athletes better “on the field” and in the classroom.
>
> The FirstString Protein mix/shake is a 3rd-party-tested and lactose-free (as are all our products) food product that we have the youth athletes (any active youth) we work with use for a pre- and post-activity shake for energy and recovery, and/or weight/muscle gain. Below is more information, including incorporating FirstString into daily planning along with a multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) to also aid recovery and fill important nutrient gaps common to youngsters. If a child has diet restrictions on animal protein, they would use the Best Plant Protein – Vegan product. I have also cc’d [NAME] here in case you have any questions.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great night.
>
> Youth/Athlete Growth, Development, Performance, Recovery & Health Dietary Support Plan
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
> KidMV (ages 2–11 years): 2–4 years take 1; 5–11 years take 2; ages 12–17 years use ActiveMV and Take-1 daily with a meal until age 18; then take 2 daily, one with the AM meal and one with the PM meal.
>
> Daily as needed:
> FirstString
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 g of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 4–5 times daily, which includes the pre/post-activity shake.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein and calorie needs for weight/muscle gain needs.
>
> SuperCalcium (read all food and shake labels): Using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with other typical diet calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing.
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days
> FirstString* — use as directed to get:
> 21 g/protein (2 scoops) 30–40 minutes before a workout and repeat the same dose immediately post-exercise.
>
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. It is better not to use any stimulants (ThermAccel has a thermogenic/stimulant blend) in someone who has high blood pressure and is using beta-blockers unless a doctor has approved it. The Weight Loss & Liver Support (WLLS) product is a non-stimulant body-fat reduction support product that should be fine, but make sure the doctor signs off (they usually do).
>
> If necessary, you can give the doctor this link to the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG), which is used for training and certification purposes only (not consumer-facing), and click on WeightLoss&LiverSupport to get the peer-reviewed science related to all ingredients in the WLLS. Below, we also placed the WLLS in the meal replacement/substitute program, as it is successfully used for body-fat reduction.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day.
>
> Level-1 for Fast Results & Daily Recovery with Meal Replacement/Substitute Integration Program
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender and follow label directions. If 50 years or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women's; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 years if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 years use Kids; children 12–17 years use 1-Active. Vegans use 1-Vegan.
>
> Weight Loss & Liver Support
> Take 3 daily as directed: 1 before each of 3 meals or evenly split throughout the day.
>
> LeanMR (protein- and fiber-rich meal replacement formula)
> Use as directed within daily meal planning (see below) and to make sure you get approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, divided 4–5 times daily depending on preference.
>
> LeanMR can also be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired ingredients) to shore up total daily protein needs. See below for proper menu integration.
>
> Meal Replacement/Substitute Integration
> You may also click here for a simple ready-to-go solution, including sample menus with proper calories, foods, and LeanMR meal replacement diet integration.
>
> Weight Loss Phase:
> Meal replacements (MRs) generally supply two small meals a day and allow freedom of choice from traditional foods for the remaining allotted foods/calories. Therefore, use as two small meals within any calorie-restricted meal plan of 4–5 meals, since frequent meals are often better for body-fat/weight reduction than fewer larger ones, especially as it relates to satiety, preservation of lean body mass (LBM), and energy levels. LeanMR recipes are available here.
>
> Maintenance Phase
> Continue to consume 4–5 small meals daily within the caloric allotment that includes 2 MRs for convenience and to help ensure overall diet quality while reducing food costs. A wide variety of healthy recipes, including meal replacement recipes, are available here.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> BrainHealth is a preventative formula that delivers the nutrients the brain needs as we age but no longer can acquire in the proper amounts. It certainly would not hurt your coach, but in the bigger picture I would include a daily 5 gm dose of CreatineMonohydrate, as it is commonly used to help heal concussed athletes. Therefore, all in all, if it fits his budget, I would have him use the program below to support his brain functioning and overall health. He can use the ActiveMV instead of the Alln1 SuperBlendTM if he has a budget concern, but the Alln1 SuperBlendTM also contains the extra dotFIT health products beyond the multivitamin, such as the SuperiorAntioxidant, UltraProbiotic, Digestive Enzymes, along with the herbs that also support brain function, especially the mushroom blend.
>
> Keep me updated on his progress and have him see his doctor regularly for the proper medical advice.
>
> Thanks.
>
> Daily:
> - Alln1 SuperBlendTM (SB)
>   Take as directed: first 2 weeks, ½ serving daily; third week, full serving daily. It may be taken all at once or ½ in the morning and ½ in the evening. Visit the recipe section to incorporate as desired into your daily lifestyle.
> - Vitamin D (to achieve a serum 25(OH)D concentration of 40–50 ng/ml)
>   Take daily with SB or anytime.
> - CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy
>   Mix 1 scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of your favorite fluid or shake and take 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal or drink for the first 5 days. Thereafter, take 1 scoop once daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals or drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>   Training days: Take 1 scoop before or after your workout with meals or drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>   Non-training days: Take 1 scoop with a meal or shake.
>
> Daily as needed:
> - Favorite dotFIT protein mix (WheySmooth, All Natural WheySmooth, and the all-vegan BestPlantProtein are most popular and cover all diet types—all are lactose-free).
>   Use as directed to make sure you get approximately 1 gm of protein per pound of lean body mass, or per pound of body weight if not overweight, daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>   If exercising, use as directed to get approximately 25–35 gm of protein 30–40 minutes before your workout and repeat the dose immediately post-workout.
>   Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself, or mixed with desired supporting contents, to shore up total daily protein needs.
> - SuperOmega-3
>   Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz per week of fatty fish. Take 1 if consuming more than 8 oz per week but less than 16 oz; take 2 if consuming 8 oz or less. Not necessary if consuming at least 16 oz per week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
> - Advanced Brain Health (may divide evenly or take all at once anytime, with or without meals)
>   45–55 years: take 4; 56+: take 8.
>
> Workout days:
> - dotFIT Protein of choice. Use as directed to get 25–35 gm of protein 30–40 minutes before your workout and repeat the same dose immediately following exercise.
> - May substitute a favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> The most popular way of consuming SB daily is mixing it with water and 25 gm of vanilla WheySmooth.
>
> Nutrition facts based on 1 scoop of WS and 1 scoop of Alln1 SuperBlendTM:
> 25 gm protein, 16.5 gm carbohydrates, 4 gm fat, 352 mg calcium, 224 mg potassium, and 195 calories.
>
> Full day's recommendation for vitamins and minerals, antioxidants, probiotics, prebiotics, Ashwagandha, turmeric and mushroom blend, along with 6 gm of fiber and 2 full servings of vegetables.
>
> As desired, add any fruit for the perfect one-and-done health habit.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Yes, your dotFIT Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM) is fine, as there is generally a smell (and color differences) that our vitamins give off because of the natural ingredients. >
> Thanks again, and have a great New Year.

> Q: I noticed that my dotFIT vitamins often look different and some have a stronger smell than others. Is this normal?
> A: Yes, all dotFIT multivitamin and mineral formulas are slightly different in looks, primarily because of the choline addition and new enteric coating. To a lesser degree, on coloring and smell, all multiple vitamin and mineral (MVM) products that contain natural ingredients such as ours (and don’t use artificial coatings) will have changing distinct features depending on ingredient harvesting and seasons, including colors and even smell, just like the plants they come from in nature. All our MVMs also use a delayed-release feature so that your body absorbs and uses the ingredients more efficiently, and we recently upgraded this, which also altered the coloring. At the end of the day, because our dietary supplements are in the 3rd-party-tested Practitioner product category, everything on the label is accurate no matter the color or taste, and they follow the science—i.e., continually updated.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We placed below the proper program, including mealtimes, that we give all parents for their growing and developing children—especially for their growing athletes. Below this are product descriptions from our lectures. After using this Level 1 program to maximize training-induced muscle and growth, if he wants to continue competing, we can take him to the Level 2 youth athlete program.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day.
>
> Youth/Athlete Growth, Development, Performance, Recovery & Health Dietary Support Plan
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Keep in mind all programs we create not only help prevent injury, speed recovery, and improve training/exercise-induced results, but also contribute to better overall health while maximizing growth and development potential. Proper nutrition makes our children and athletes better “on the field” and in the classroom.
>
> The FirstString protein mix/shake is a third-party-tested and lactose-free food product that we have the youth athletes (any active youth) we work with use for a pre- and post-activity shake for energy and recovery, and/or weight/muscle gain. Below is more information, including incorporating FirstString into daily planning along with a multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) to also aid recovery and fill important nutrient gaps common to youngsters. If the child has dietary restrictions on animal protein, use the Best Plant Protein—Vegan product.
>
> Daily:
> Ages 12–17 use ActiveMV and Take-1 daily with a meal until age 18; then take 2 daily, one with the AM meal and one with the PM meal.
>
> Daily as needed:
> FirstString
> Use as directed to make sure you get approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 5 times daily.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein and calorie needs for weight/muscle gain needs.
>
> SuperCalcium (read all food and shake labels; using FirstString or other dotFIT protein powder regularly with other typical diet calcium intake would probably meet calcium needs without supplementing).
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (approximately 1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal. Males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if more than 8 oz/week but less than 16 oz; 2 if 8 oz or less); not necessary if consuming at least 16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days
> FirstString*—use as directed to get:
> 25–35 g of protein 30–40 minutes before the workout and repeat the same dose immediately post-exercise.
>
> *May substitute a favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> Meal Timings
> As possible, eat every 4 hours.
>
> Large pre-training/event meal 2–3 hours before training.
>
> Large post-meal approximately 30–60 minutes after the last post-workout supplement.
>
> Early morning training
> Eat a large pre-training-type meal the night before.
>
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) and follow the workout-day supplement schedule above.
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
> Less than 1.5 hours break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink.
>
> 1.5–2.5 hours: small pre-training-type meal.
>
> More than 2.5 hours: normal pre-training meal.
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
> 16 oz 2 hours before activity (an extra 8–16 oz 1 hour before on hot days).
>
> 4–8 oz every 20 minutes during activity.
>
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity.
>
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g., Gatorade) and water as directed.

>

>
> Creatine Monohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy (if you are under 175 lbs, you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half*—i.e., after the loading period, take a total of 5 g daily and split as described below)
>
> Mix 1 scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first 5 days. Thereafter, take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> Training days: take 1 dose before workout and 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> Non-training days*: take 1 dose with AM meal and 1 dose with PM meal.
>
> *Program’s total daily creatine: first 5 days, 20 g/day; maintenance phase, all days 5–10 g. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day with some protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meals or shakes.
>

>
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 g of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content)
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with AM meal and the second with PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> Super Omega 3
>
> Take 1–2 daily with meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> Workout days
> AminoFormula
> Take 1 scoop ~5–10 minutes before and may continue to drink during workout.
> Take 1 scoop immediately following workout.
>
> Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10 minutes before workout and consume at a pace so it’s finished at end of activity.
>
> Creatine Monohydrate
> On training days, use one dose before workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Collagen protein is not a complete protein (it lacks tryptophan and cysteine and is low in essential amino acids (EAA) necessary for muscle protein synthesis) and therefore is not used in total muscle recovery. Collagen is used for skin and joint health and is often added to a complete protein such as whey, which is used for muscle protein synthesis (MPS). Your best solution for recovery, including joint and skin health, is to take your dotFIT JointSkinCollagen+ daily and use your favorite dotFIT protein, such as WheySmooth, as your muscle protein supplement. **Source answer:**

> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. There is a lot of junk science regarding methylated vitamin forms, so we seem to be getting this question often. See below for your answer and be mindful that only B9 (folate) and B12 are available in the market in their methylated forms, and the body will convert forms as needed depending on the destination regardless of origin, as long as you have enough. The Alln1 SuperBlend (and dotFIT MVMs) uses both B9/Folate (calcium L-5-methyltetrahydrofolate) and B12 (methylcobalamin) in the methylated forms.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> Q: Are all your vitamins methylated vitamins? How would I be able to tell?
> A: Your question is vague, in the fact that not all vitamins should contain a methyl donor to be effective (actually, it is a problem for some). What you want in any vitamin is an absorbable form (i.e., a form that is already prepared as the body would prepare it once extracted from a food source). All our vitamins (and minerals) are in a pre-formed structure to allow the body proper “functional absorption” (active at the target tissue), which is better than bioavailable absorption. All our labels have the form listed. Do not be fooled that all vitamins need a methyl group to be effective. In all our products, if it needs a methyl group, it will be part of it and listed on the label, such as B12 or Folate in our Alln1 SuperBlendTM. If you would like to talk to a chemist further, please reach back out and [NAME] (VP of Product Development) will connect you.
>
> From: [EMAIL]
> Sent: Thursday, October 10, 2024 12:57 PM
> To: Experts <[EMAIL]>
> Subject: Contact Us
>
> Hey buddy,
>
> Something is wrong with whatever your trainer is referring to. He is reading (or hearing from an unqualified influencer) something out of context. Both cyanocobalamin and methylcobalamin are forms the body uses to functionally absorb B12 (arrive at target tissues and go to work), which is why both forms are often used in Multivitamin and Mineral Formulas (MVM). The Alln1 SuperBlendTM only uses methylcobalamin to keep it all vegan. Your trainer is confused by stuff floating around the internet about “methylation”. If he has a series of peer review groundbreaking studies supporting what he thinks he is saying that uses human subjects consuming normal physiologic dosages of B12, please send them with an explanation regarding the methods and materials used to support the conclusion.
>
> In the meantime, he can read some important facts: Cyanocobalamin is a synthetic compound of vitamin B12 used to treat vitamin deficiencies. Chemically, cyanocobalamin is classified as a "corrinoid," representing a crystallizable cobalt complex. The name "cyanocobalamin" is derived from including a cyanide group within the molecule. Cyanocobalamin is approved by the United States Food and Drug Administration (FDA) for pernicious anemia, malabsorption, atrophic gastritis, gastrectomy, Helicobacter pylori infection, and other conditions. Vitamin B12 facilitates several methylation reactions within the body. Methylcobalamin is a cofactor that helps in the conversion of homocysteine to methionine in the body. Furthermore, in the form of adenosylcobalamin, the vitamin is crucial in converting methylmalonyl-coenzyme A (CoA) to succinyl-CoA. Both of these reactions are essential for cell division and growth. Therefore, Vitamin B12 in two forms: methylcobalamin and cyanocobalamin, are important but methylcobalamin is the most effective at being delivered to neurons to support brain health.
>
> See below for more layman confusion, mindful that only B9 (folate) and B12 are available in the market in their methylated forms and the body will generally convert forms as needed depending on final destination regardless of origin, as long as you have enough.
>
> Q: Are all your vitamins methylated vitamins? How would I be able to tell?
> A: Your question is vague, in the fact that not all vitamins should contain a methyl donor to be effective (actually, it is a problem for some). What you want in any vitamin is an absorbable form (i.e., a form that is already prepared as the body would prepare it once extracted from a food source). All our vitamins (and minerals) are in a pre-formed structure to allow the body proper “functional absorption” (active at the target tissue), which is better than bioavailable absorption. All our labels have the form listed. Do not be fooled that all vitamins need a methyl group to be effective. In all our products, if it needs a methyl group, it will be part of it and listed on the label, such as B12 or Folate in our Alln1 SuperBlendTM. If you would like to talk to a chemist further, please reach back out and [NAME] (VP of Product Development) will connect you.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The good news is that your member is not an expert, so no one should listen to him on this topic. First of all, many people don’t supplement anything and they are not copper deficient—meaning there is no way the body figures out how to make it automatically 15:2. The body uses what you give it and balances its needs based on levels (the triage factor). The goal is to have all the body can use by supplementing what we know the body doesn’t get from food. That’s what the Alln1 SuperBlendTM and our pill products do perfectly. Refer your member to the PDSRG Vitamin sections (see below). Ironically (and because we want it this way because of normal food intake), the zinc-to-copper ratio in the Alln1 SuperBlendTM is about 15:2—12 mg (12,000 µg) zinc; copper is 800 µg (0.8 gm). Therefore, 12,000:800 is approximately 15:2.
>
> No amount of zinc below the RDA will remove the body’s copper.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great weekend.
>
> The daily amounts of vitamins and minerals currently known as the safe and optimal range are captured in the Practitioner’s Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) and contained within those documented amounts in the dotFIT formulas—the design of the Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM).

**Source answer:**

> Hey [CUSTOMER],
>
> Nothing more than a California labeling law thing so bottom-feeder attorneys can’t file frivolous lawsuits. It’s been in play for years for products sold in California, but you can’t separate inventories, so it’s easier to put it on most labels, since it’s virtually meaningless and on buildings (in fine print) that sell food in California (more naturally occurring lead in 2 leaves of vegetables than any NSF-Certified supplement 😊). Like all our products, MuscleDefender (Glutamine) meets and exceeds all federal and global standards for purity, including parts per million (PPM) of naturally occurring lead content, but California chose to have a warning on all products regardless of meeting federal and global, including NSF, standards.
>
> See the link below. It includes everything you need to know (and more) about Prop 65. Mindful, it ONLY applies to California, and it’s nothing more than a CYA labeling issue, including being posted in all California grocery stores, coffee shops, etc. (unless you are a small company, then you are exempt, which of course makes no sense). There is also an FAQ at the very bottom (once in the link) for a quick answer. Sadly, many companies have had to defend lawsuits for simply not including the Proposition notice on the label—even though the products have met all federal and global standards for naturally occurring compounds.
>
> Always good to hear from you, buddy. And enjoy your MuscleDefender, just as the rest of our clients and families do regularly!
> https://www.dotfit.com/prop65

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> See below for ideal full stacking (I also attached a slide). You can alter it to your liking as far as how you consume everything, as long as the doses and timing are maintained. If you have already started using creatine, just use the maintenance dose shown in the chart (5–10 g/day). If you only need 5 g because you are under 175 LBS, just use the CreatineXXXL/NO7 daily to maintain creatine levels. The only time you would add CreatineMonohydrate alone would be if you are over 175 LBS; otherwise, CreatineXXXL twice daily (or CreatineXXXL once and NO7 once) will supply your full creatine needs.
>
> Hope this helps.
>
> Adult Super Stack Performance & Size Supplement Plan (NO7Rage, ExtremeCreatineXXXL & CM)
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Active Multivitamin Mineral Formula
> Take two (2) daily: one (1) immediately following the first large meal and one (1) after the final meal of the day.
>
> SuperOmega-3
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16 oz; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> FirstString (or WheySmooth depending on daily calorie allotment)
> Take two (2) scoops 30–45 minutes before a workout, or use a favorite dotFIT® bar for convenience.
>
> Take two (2) scoops 20–30 minutes after the post-workout AminoFormula dose.
>
> Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself, mixed with desired ingredients, to add protein/calories as needed to meet daily requirements.
>
> Be sure to ingest approximately one (1) g of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 4–5 times daily along with the pre- and post-workout shakes. The pre- and post-workout shakes combined generally supply 50–80 g of the daily requirement.
>
> AminoFormula (AF) (workout days only)
> Take one scoop approximately 10 minutes before a workout (add to NO7Rage if using this product—see below). You may continue to drink it during the workout.
>
> Take one (1) scoop immediately following the workout.
>
> Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10 minutes before the workout, consuming it at a pace so it is finished at the end of the activity.
>
> Begin Creatine Supplementation at the 2nd Week of the Program
>
> 2nd Week: Start CreatineMonohydrate (CrM)*
> Loading phase: Mix one (1) scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of a favorite fluid/shake and take four (4) times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first five (5) days.
>
> On training days, use one dose before the workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> Maintenance phase: After the five (5)-day loading phase:
>
> On training days, take one (1) scoop daily with the post-workout shake.
>
> On non-training days, take 1 scoop with any meal when not taking ECX (i.e., use ECX at different times so total creatine is evenly dispersed throughout the day).
>
> Persons <175 LBS can discontinue CrM dosing when starting ECX, because ECX daily dosing will maintain CrM stores.
>
> Add ExtremeCreatineXXXL (ECX) on the 6th day of the 2nd Week
> Two (2) scoops supply 3.2 g of beta-alanine (BA), 5 g of creatine monohydrate, and 7 g of L-glutamine.
>
> Take 2 scoops daily. Take with some protein and/or carbohydrates, but within allotted calories based on body composition goal.
>
> Training days: Take one (1) scoop before training with the pre-workout full meal (generally 2–3 hours pre-workout). If not possible based on early training, take with the pre-workout shake. Take the remaining dose (1 scoop) anytime with a meal or shake when not taking a CreatineMonohydrate dose to help evenly spread total creatine intake throughout the day.
>
> Non-training days: Take 1 scoop with the morning meal or shake and 1 scoop with the evening meal or shake.
>
> Add NO7Rage at the 4th week (workout days only)
> Take 1.5–2.5 scoops depending on body weight and caffeine sensitivity—start with 1.5 and increase to the recommended dose if not uncomfortably affected by caffeine—approximately 10 minutes before the workout. May mix with AF and continue to consume during the workout. See directions on the label for weight dosages.
>
> NO7Rage contains 150 mg of caffeine per scoop. As a reference, Starbucks Grande drip coffee contains approximately 330 mg of caffeine, close to the same amount in two (2) scoops of NO7Rage.
>
> Total daily creatine for this plan: Loading phase, 20 g/day. Thereafter until the fourth week, 10 g/day (or 5 g if <175 LBS). At the fourth week, on workout days, 12.5–15 g/day and 10 g on non-workout days (half this if <175 LBS). Total BA intake is 6.2–8.2 g/day on workout days and 3.2 g on non-workout days. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day and around the workout as described, and ingested with some carbohydrates and/or protein.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We do not make other flavors of CreatineMonohydrate (CM). Many people will put CM in their smoothies or pre/post workout formulas, such as the dotFIT proteins and AminoFormula, for different flavors, which is also why we keep a relatively neutral flavor for our CM. See below for a related FAQ and more information. Further, our licensed facilities have limited room on their shelves.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day.
>
> Q: Will you make more flavors of ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ or CreatineMonohydrate?
> A: We probably won’t make other flavor extensions of ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (ECX) or CreatineMonohydrate (CM) in the near future, primarily because the demand is not high enough to justify a full run, including the NSF-CS testing, and our facilities only have so much shelf space for supplements, so we must pick our battles. Most people do mix it in their shakes, including adding it to many different flavored smoothies. Further, because the flavor is mild, it complements other products when mixed with them, such as the different flavors of the AminoFormula, which is commonly mixed with ECX.
>
> See below for more information on flavor and product extensions.
>
> More Info
>
> We do not make another flavored ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (ECX) or CreatineMonohydrate because most of our facilities can carry only so much inventory (see below for more on making product flavor extensions or other niche products). ECX is purposely the current flavor so that it blends with any of the pre- and post-workout proteins and the different flavored AminoFormulas, since most people will mix it with those powders. The mild raspberry flavor also allows it to be mixed with juices of preference if you would like to alter the taste.
>
> The only reason we don't come out with products the way everyone would want them produced—and there are many requests—is because, at this point in time, within our sport/fitness practitioner product distribution channel, we don't have a large enough respective market to produce a niche product or flavor in the quantities necessary to be even slightly competitive with currently available specialty product lines that cater to unique markets. You must produce 10s of thousands at one time of each product to be price competitive, as we do for all other products we sell, with the additional cost of 3rd-party/NSF testing. Doing this with a unique, requested product or flavor, because our market for that specialty product or flavor is limited, would mean most of the product would expire before we sold it, leaving a huge waste and lost revenues.
>
> When the market increases for other flavors, sizes, or particular products driven by our practitioner distribution channel, we will add them as necessary. Moreover, our licensed outlets only have so much room for inventory, making them unable to carry many different flavors or sizes of the same product and therefore only carrying top sellers.

>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> *The FDA established an acceptable daily intake (ADI) for sucralose of 5 milligrams per kilogram (Europe is 7 and Canada 11 mg/kg) of body weight (mg/kg per day). The ADI represents an amount 100 times less than the quantity of sucralose found to be safe in research studies. For a person weighing 150 pounds (68 kg), the US ADI equates to 340 mg of sucralose—the amount found in nine cans of diet soda or more than 28 individual packets of sucralose—consumed, on average, every day over a lifetime.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The dotFIT BrainHealth supplement (BH) is different than brain/memory supplements that claim to improve memory. The BH supplement is designed to protect the brain from general age-related cognitive decline, which is why the goal is to start using it around age 45 when normal brain nutrition (see ingredients in BH) is compromised from natural aging, including digestion and diet. Further, the reason you see an increase in servings by age is because, as we age, we become more resistant to brain nutrition. Thus, increasing it according to science may continue to stave off age-related cognitive decline. In summary, the BH is used to keep your brain intact, including preserving memory, by feeding it all it can use. Whereas the so-called memory enhancers, which are not evidence-based, claim to bring your memory back (and they generally do not contain brain nutrition).
>
> At your age, the dosage of the BH can stop at 8 tablets per day rather than going to 12, since we changed the formula to a tableted form (the label still says 3 servings after 65, but it shouldn’t be necessary now—it won’t hurt either). The new label, in a couple of months, will reflect the 1- and 2-serving age amounts, leaving off the third. I placed some data below from the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG), which is used for staff education and certification (not consumer-facing) regarding the BH. Most importantly for you would be to make sure your brain is being fed properly, remain active, and challenge your brain regularly.
>
> Hope this helps.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day.
>
> From the PDSRG
>
> Advanced Brain Health™ Formula
>
> Goal
>
> To supply nutrition that’s often limited by typical diets and the inevitable factors of the natural aging process, and known to support healthy brain function, including potentially reducing the risk of age-related cognitive decline.
>
> Rationale
>
> Until late in the 20th century, the basic theory was that we enter adult life with a set number of brain cells that deteriorate gradually until brain function falls apart. During the past 10 years, neuroscientists have proved that this does not need to be the case. The brain does continue to form new connections and to make changes in response to new demands. Like most every other part of the body, the brain is a “use it or lose it” organ. This fact not only means performing regular brain activities but also includes physical activity because exercise helps the brain maintain structural integrity through increased blood flow. Despite this new knowledge, it is common for people to experience overall decline in brain function with age. This tendency may be influenced by reduced use of the brain, sedentary lifestyles, and by common limitations in brain nutrition.
>
> Several substances are showing the potential to support brain function and to slow (and possibly stop or reverse) age-related decline in mental function. Among these substances, phosphatidylserine (PS), acetyl-L-carnitine (ALC), alpha-lipoic acid (ALA), and vitamin B-12 have been found to offer support to the maintenance of aging brain function. Following the middle-age years, supplementation with these compounds may balance a decline in the body’s production or absorption of these substances that are essential for normal brain and neurological function. Clinical findings support the benefit of nutritional supplements for cognitive performance and mood/behavior and suggest that additional supplementation may be required for the elderly.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Yes, calcium carbonate is considered the better form of calcium, partially because of cost and bioavailability with food. See below for more information from the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG). Also be mindful that the SuperCalcium contains the necessary cofactors (e.g., Vitamin D, magnesium, and both forms of vitamin K) to properly deposit the mineral in bone. And it’s NSF-Certified for Sport.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> More Info
>
> The two common forms of calcium supplements are calcium citrate and carbonate, with properly prepared calcium carbonate showing superior absorption when ingested with food, which is when we recommend taking a calcium supplement—i.e., take with meals. The carbonate form also has the highest content of calcium by weight, allowing less expense and a smaller pill size containing more calcium than citrate and other forms.38F Calcium carbonate is 40% calcium by weight, while calcium citrate is only 21%.39 Further, calcium citrate may inhibit absorption of non-heme iron, while other forms, including carbonate, show little to no effect. However, calcium citrate may have a clinical application since it does not require food-stimulated acids for digestion.

> Hi [NAME],

> Thanks for contacting us. The Lean Pak90 is designed to last 90 days by using each product for 30 days and following the diet information, but many people do use them simultaneously. See below for more information including Common FAQs.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> FAQs
>
> Q: Can I take all Lean Pack 90 products daily or individually over the 90 days?
> A: You can use the products as they are designed for the kit, meaning using the Weight Loss and Liver Support the first 30 days; CarbRepel the second 30 days, and end with ThermAccel for the last 30 days for a total of 90 days. Or you can use them simultaneously if you need extra assistance or have a tight timeline to lose weight since they all work by different mechanisms. Simply use each one as directed daily.
>
> Q: Do you recommend CR (or WLLS) and ThermAccel used together if I want to boost weight/fat loss?
> A: Yes, you can use CarbRepel with ThermAccel (TA), or any of the dotFIT weight loss products such as Weight Loss and Liver Support together, and it is commonly done with people needing extra help, as each product works to support appetite and metabolism through different and unique mechanisms. Simply follow the directions on each product label. The links will take you to a brief product description including a short video.
>
> Q: A client asked me if she should still take CarbRepel (CR) before a meal if not eating carbs at that particular meal. Also, what if you forget to take it 30 minutes before a meal? Can you take it right before or should you wait until later?
> A: No, you would not use CR if not consuming carbs, or very little carbs such as only vegetables or a little fruit. Taking CR as it was used in the clinical trials is ideal (~30 min before a carb-containing meal), but it will still work if you take it at, before, or very close to the meal.
>
> Q: A client asked me if she should still take WLLS in the morning if she is not going to eat breakfast. Also, what if you forget to take it 30 minutes before a meal? Can you take it right before or should you wait until later?
> A: The bottom line is that the Weight Loss & Liver Support should be consumed 3 times daily and separated as evenly as possible to help maintain the desired levels of ingredients. Taking it before a meal is one way of spreading it out and getting help in appetite support within 30 min. But it is not necessary once you start using the product regularly. The goal is to keep it consistent, so it works all day, because each dose brings the levels back up to maximum effectiveness.
>
> And yes, have your client take it in the morning even if not consuming breakfast.
>
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The Lean Pak90 is designed to last 90 days by using each product for 30 days, but many people do use them simultaneously. See below for more information including Common FAQs.
>
> Thanks again and have a great weekend.

> You do not have to load creatine again after taking time off unless you want the full ergogenic effect sooner. After 28–35 days of the maintenance dose only, you will be fully loaded again. >
> Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ as a Standalone Product
> • Take two scoops daily (5 g creatine, 3.2 g beta-alanine, 7 g L-glutamine) to achieve immediate training benefits and reach near maximum creatine and beta-alanine supplemented levels within 28–35 days, and continue throughout the desired training period (always take with some protein and carbs, but within allotted calories based on body composition goal).
> • Training days: take 1 scoop before training and one after. May mix in your pre- and post-workout shake containing protein with some carbohydrate.
> • Non-training days: take 1 scoop with morning meal or shake and one (1) scoop with evening meal or shake.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The FirstString Protein is what athletes/exercisers use to improve muscle size and performance because it contains the exact same whey protein as WheySmooth but has the additional calories and carbohydrate (CHO) content per serving to support the energy requirement necessary for intense workouts that lead to increases in muscle and performance, which includes better overall exercise recovery. See below for more information. That said, you can still use the WheySmooth and add your choice of carbohydrates/calories (e.g., fruits, juices, specialized CHO mixtures, etc.; see the recipe section) to your daily shake. I also placed a note regarding monitoring muscle gain below, including ideal mealtimes.
>
> Hope this helps. Thanks again and have a great evening.
>
> Muscle/weight gain
>
> Total daily calorie intake should be moderately above current expenditure, such as 250–500 cals (the dotFIT program will automatically create the right menus, and choose the “Athletic Menu” to use as a guideline). If weight gain does not occur as described, you may add roughly 100 to 250 calories to your daily total (based on body size), consisting of equal amounts of carbohydrates and protein and moderate fat. For example, 20 g protein, 20 g carbohydrate, and 10 g fat, equaling 250 calories. If preferred, use your shakes or snacks to supply extra calories. Following the addition of extra calories, if after one week weight gain does not occur, repeat the above process*.
>
> All this is automatically taken care of using your dotFIT program.
>
> *If body fat or overall weight increases undesirably, slightly reduce daily caloric intake, or add a preferred form of aerobic exercise until you achieve your desired weight trend.
>
> Staying within the calories conducive to your body composition goal, below are the ideal mealtimes to maximize performance.
>
> Meal Timings
> - As possible, eat every 3–4 hours.
> - Larger pre-training/event meal: 2–3 hr before training.
> - Larger post meal: approximately 30–60 min after last post-workout supplement.
>
> Early morning training
> - Eat a large pre-training-type meal the night before.
> - Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) and follow the workout day supplement schedule above.
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
> - <1.5 hr break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink.
> - 1.5–2.5 hr: small pre-training-type meal.
> - >2.5 hr: normal pre-training meal.
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
> - 16 oz 2 hr before activity (extra 8–16 oz 1 hr before on hot days).
> - 4–8 oz every 20 minutes during activity.
> - 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity.
>
> *Use electrolyte formula (e.g., Gatorade) and water as directed.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We are not sure what you are asking here, other than there is no evidence that chromium supplementation at a proper daily amount* is toxic anywhere in the body. If you are referring to higher doses (we can’t tell a dose you are referring to from what you wrote), the answer is that any metal/mineral can be toxic at high levels—i.e., daily ingestion is regularly above the Tolerable Upper Limit Levels (UL)—and chromium would be no exception. That said, chromium up to 1000 mcg daily is likely safe for short-term use, and doses of 200–1000 mcg daily have been used safely for up to 2 years. And there are no established Tolerable Upper Limit Levels for chromium at this time.
>
> Hope this helps—let us know if you have any questions.
>
> Have a great weekend.
>
> *Chromium Adequate Intake (AI): The AI is an estimated amount of chromium that is generally considered sufficient for most people.
>
> Men (19–50 years): 35 mcg
> Women (19–50 years): 25 mcg
> Older adults (51+ years): 30 mcg for men and 20 mcg for women

- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We don’t think much of L-carnitine as a dietary supplement for fitness goals, including performance or body fat reduction, in healthy athletes/exercisers. L-carnitine is used clinically with success in cardiovascular-compromised humans (e.g., deficiency states, some cardiovascular/metabolic settings, and nerve/neuropathy contexts). If your goals are fat loss or acute performance, carnitine is not a primary driver. Proper energy balance, protein, creatine, beta-alanine, caffeine, etc. (i.e., evidence-based dietary supplements), progressive overload, and sleep will wash out any benefit of carnitine supplementation. Carnitine can be a nice-to-have for recovery in high dosages (2–4 g/day) and may nudge weight metrics downward over months—especially in higher-BMI clients—as long as the rest of the plan is tight.
>
> Hope this helps.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great Sunday night.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. First, we are non-clinical in recommendations, meaning our protocols are to help prevent the bad things that happen when you do not consume from your foods the nutrition your body can use to be healthy and resist aging. That said, your blood-thinning medications (warfarin) should not interfere with a baseline dietary support program (including the correct dose of vitamin K) unless your doctor knows something we do not about your unique physiology—so make sure you inform your doctor of anything you decide to incorporate into your daily routine. Creatine is also a great addition to your baseline program. There should be no need for NAD or NMD when you are following a complete metabolism reboot program such as below. You can skip the creatine loading phase and just start with the maintenance, which is 5–10 gm/d based on weight. ≥175 LBS use 10 gm/d; <175 use 5 gm.
>
> Thanks again and have a great evening, ([NAME] is CC’d here in case you need more information.)
>
> General Sport Health, Activity Recovery, Performance Program – CM in Baseline
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
>
> DotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 yrs or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 yrs if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 yr use Kids; children 12–17 yr use 1-Active.
>
> Take as directed with meals.
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance Strategy: If you are under 175 LBS, you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half*—i.e., after the loading period, take a total of 5 gms daily and split as described below.
>
> Mix 1 scoop (5 gms) with 4–8 oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first 5 days. Thereafter, take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> Training days: Take 1 dose before the workout and 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> Non-training days*: Take 1 dose with the AM meal and 1 dose with the PM meal.
>
> *Program total daily creatine: first 5 days, 20 gms/d. Maintenance phase: all days, 5–10 gms. To maximize uptake, creatine intake should be spread as evenly as possible throughout the day with some protein- or carb-containing meals or shakes.
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix, based on caloric allotment for goal.
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get approximately 1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself, or mixed with desired supporting contents, to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content).
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (approximately 1,000–1,200 mgs) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal. Males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> Super Omega 3.
>
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> Workout days:
>
> Creatine Monohydrate.
>
> On training days, use one dose before the workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.

> Or use both scoops, start ingesting 10 minutes before the workout, and consume at a pace so it’s finished at the end of the activity.
>
> Non-workout days:
>
> Take 1 scoop in the morning and 1 scoop later in the day when not consuming a full protein meal.
>
> As needed:
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content, including dotFIT protein shakes)*
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (approximately 1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the morning meal and the second with the evening meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> Super Omega 3
>
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if over 8 oz/week but under 16 oz; 2 if 8 oz or less); not necessary if consuming 16 oz or more per week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us and thanks for the thoughtful questions. The amount of vitamin E in any supplement should be based on the population you serve, which is what ours does. I’m guessing you are a certified Practitioner; therefore, you realize you have all the answers to your questions related to evidence-based dosages for all dotFIT products in the Practitioner Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG). If not, I have cc’d [NAME] here to make sure you know how to get access to the science supporting proper recommendations.
>
> The quick answer is that the vitamin E in our Active and other dotFIT multivitamin and mineral formulas (MVM), including the Alln1 SuperBlend™, is in the proper daily amounts to support active individuals and does not exceed the Tolerable Upper Limit Levels (UL). The Food and Nutrition Board of the Institute of Medicine has set the UL for vitamin E at 1,000 mg (1,500 IU) per day for supplemental vitamin E. Further, you mention that few persons are vitamin E deficient (agreed—see the true definition of a deficiency, which is death if not corrected). However, most persons are vitamin E insufficient. Meaning, nothing works optimally where vitamin E is involved, which is about 300 different necessary life functions. Eighty-six percent of the U.S. population is below the RDA, and almost that much is below the EAR. Therefore, vitamin E is listed as a nutrient of concern in the Dietary Guidelines for Americans. All this makes it an important supplement for our population of active individuals. There will always be subpopulations that should not have certain amounts of specific nutrients, but that is a clinical issue, and they should know this from their health history. We are on the protective and daily recovery side with the appropriate amounts of supplemental MVMs to correct the insufficiencies of Western diets for our population.
>
> Below is more information, including a friendly reminder of the population we work with, which is satisfying the nutrition requirements of humans who want to remain healthy and active. We own this domain along with sports performance and fitness performance.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great weekend.
>
> More info.
>
> The vitamin and mineral amounts in all the dotFIT formulas are the best in science today, unless you have an unusual contraindicated medical condition, since they were designed by vitamin and mineral researchers to complement the Western diets in industrialized nations. In other words, they are designed not just to fill gaps but also to offer protection against the common maladies of aging. With all due respect to the studies you may cite, they simply call out an association that does not apply to the general active population.
>
> The bottom line is that the nutrient RDAs in the DRIs, including Daily Values, are set and designed to correct deficiencies and are not meant to be optimal. Moreover, the safe and optimal range is well above the RDAs for many vitamins, such as C, D, E, and the B vitamins, as well as some minerals, but never over the Tolerable Upper Intake Levels (UL), which is the end range. See the chart in your PDSRG.
>
> The daily amounts of vitamins and minerals currently known as the safe and optimal range are captured in the peer-reviewed Practitioner’s Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) and contained within those documented amounts in the dotFIT formulas—the design of the Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM).
>
> More information on vitamin and mineral daily intakes above the RDAs
>
> Current evidence-based supplemental amounts of vitamins and minerals for active individuals that support long- and short-term health are contained in the dotFIT formulas, including the Alln1 SuperBlend™, especially the primary antioxidant vitamins C and E. Further, none of the ingredients are at or over the known ULs; they are all in the known established safe and optimal range. The complete rationale for each ingredient is from the world’s leading experts and found in the Practitioner’s Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG), which is used for certification and practitioner education, not consumer-facing education. All references are from peer-reviewed published research, and the formulas are third-party tested, with most having an additional NSF Certified for Sport designation, meaning they also meet all worldwide safety, label-claim, and drug-testing regulations. The product is formulated by the experts at dotFIT. Feel free to send the information links to your customer or dietitian. The content will be a good education for the inquisitive person. Have your contact click on the appropriate links that explain the design of the Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM), and then they can click on links to the peer-reviewed referenced article that sets the foundation found in the PDSRG document.

> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.

> Daily:
> ActiveMV
> Take 2 daily, one with the AM meal and one with the PM meal.
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading & Maintenance* Strategy
> Mix 1 scoop (5 grams) with 4–8 oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first 5 days. Thereafter, take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrates.
>
> Training days: 1 dose before the workout and 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> Non-training days: Take 1 dose with the AM meal and 1 dose with the PM meal.
>
> Daily as needed:
> FirstString
> Use as directed to make sure you get approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including food, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> If exercising, use approximately 25 grams 30–40 minutes before the workout and repeat the same dose immediately following exercise.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> Super Omega 3
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz). Not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes; then increase as directed.
>
> Workout days
> FirstString
> Take as directed (25 grams of protein) 30–40 minutes before the workout and repeat immediately post-exercise.
>
> Creatine Monohydrate
> On training days, use one dose before the workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> Ideal mealtimes to maximize performance (keep meals within calories conducive to body composition goals)
>
> Meal Timings
> As possible, eat every 4 hours.
> Larger pre-training/event meal 2–3 hours before training.
> Larger post-workout meal approximately 30–60 minutes after the last post-workout supplement.
>
> Early morning training
> Eat a large pre-training-type meal the night before.
> Consume only the pre-workout snack/shake before training (as shown above) and follow the workout-day supplement schedule above.
>
> Tournament play (multiple games)
> <1.5-hour break: bars and hydration/electrolyte recovery drink.
> 1.5–2.5 hours: small pre-training-type meal.
> >2.5 hours: normal pre-training meal.
>
> Fluid Recommendations*
> 16 oz 2 hours before activity (an extra 8–16 oz 1 hour before on hot days).
> 4–8 oz every 20 minutes during activity.
> 20 oz for every pound of weight loss post-activity.
>
> *If necessary (extended training sessions), use electrolyte formula and water as directed; may mix with AminoFormula.

## G-158 [test] 2025 — Active MV

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. We will help you lose that belly fat, mindful that no matter how much you work out or what exercises you perform, you won’t get rid of belly fat without a proper diet and there is no such thing as spot reducing (see note). You need to support your muscle (LBM) while losing body fat, including from the belly area. Assuming you are not a member of one of our licensed facilities, I’m connecting you with [NAME] (cc’d here), one of our certified counselors, to help you choose your best and healthiest route to your goals based on what can fit into your lifestyle. It will be best for you two to jump on a quick call when it is convenient for both. Following the right dotFIT program that is set up so you burn more calories than you consume, including the supplement program below, will get rid of the belly fat, support LBM, and give you the energy you need while pursuing the goal because you will be feeding your muscle while starving body fat.
>
> Thanks again and good luck on your fitness journey. We will be with you all the way!
>
> Spot reducing note
>
> Losing body fat anywhere on the body requires consuming fewer calories than you burn, regardless of the type of diet you consume. The goal is to find a diet that you’re comfortable with so you can stick with it until you reach your goal, knowing there is no such thing as “spot reducing”—i.e., fat leaves the body from wherever it’s genetically programmed to lose it, and sometimes the places you want it to leave from first do not happen until later—but it will happen if you stay in the calorie deficit. You do this by eating less, moving more, or a combination of the two until you reach the goal. While aging can alter where you lose and store fat because of hormone shifts, the formula for losing body fat anywhere on the body is the same, including eventually leaving from any place first, second, or third.
>
> Building or Maintaining Muscle While Losing Body Fat and the dotFIT Program
>
> Building or maintaining muscle while losing body fat, depending on age and training experience (younger and less experience make the gains easier and faster), is not often easy to accomplish because the formula requires a few components that you need to adhere to: 1) a daily menu plan that has the correct calories and foods, including protein quantity matched to the body composition goal; 2) a dietary supplement program to feed muscle while starving fat—i.e., deliver high nutrition with little to no calories—meaning you will need at least a multivitamin and mineral (MVM) and extra protein to help recover and maintain structural and functional integrity (when calories are limited to reduce body fat, you also limit the nutrients that would otherwise be contained in those calories); 3) regular unaccustomed resistance exercise. Additionally, we placed the minimal supplement program we use for exercisers to healthily speed recovery and results while supporting health and fat loss.
>
> Note: Make sure you consume daily, between all food and protein shake sources, close to 1 gram of protein for every pound of lean body mass (LBM), or body weight if not overweight, divided 4–5 times daily, including using your protein shake before and after the workouts. See below. As you progress, you can add additional supplementation if you continue to want to lose more body fat and/or gain more muscle by regularly updating your program with your weight/body fat measurements (it will automatically update the diet and supplements), or use our supplement screener tool: https://www.dotfit.com/supplements-screener
>
> General Health, Recovery, Weight Control/Body Fat Reduction/Toning and Performance Supplement Program
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
>
>  dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 years or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 years if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 years use Kids; children 12–17 years use 1-Active.
>
> Take as directed with meals.
>
> WheySmooth or another favorite dotFIT protein mix, based on caloric allotment for the goal.
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of LBM, or weight if not overweight, daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself, or mixed with desired supporting contents, to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> As needed:
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content, including dotFIT protein shakes)*
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (approximately 1000–1200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> Super Omega 3
>
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if more than 8 oz/week but less than 16 oz; take 2 if 8 oz or less); not necessary if consuming 16 oz or more per week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> *All food labels, including protein powders, must contain calcium levels by serving, so always check.
>
> Workout Days:
>
> WheySmooth or another favorite dotFIT protein mix.
>
> Take 1 scoop approximately 30–40 minutes before the workout.
>
> Take 1 scoop immediately following the workout.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. 500 mg/day of Ashwagandha (Withania somnifera) from the plant root is commonly used daily indefinitely (albeit no long-term data other than in India, where it is often consumed daily for a lifetime at 250–750 mg/day). In the 3 years that the Alln1 SuperBlend™ has been used regularly, blood results from our clients remain stellar related to liver, kidney, etc., health (e.g., SGOT [AST], SGPT [ALT], eGFR, etc.). If you were to use it for athletic purposes only (adaptogen with performance benefits [750–1,250 mg/day]), you may want to cycle it around your seasons. Our manufacturer does not use KSM-66 but uses an equally effective root extract (NSF-CS). Everyone is different and may have unknown underlying issues that may prohibit them from experiencing typical benefits that most apparently healthy people may from specific supplements, and therefore, regular physicals are recommended with the standard blood panels regardless.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.

**Source answer:**

> Bloating is a funny thing and it’s almost unavoidable in perimenopause and menopause, no matter what you eat. So, the first question I ask females is age. Then all you can do is play around with different foods to minimize the effects.
>
> As far as detox goes, on its face (detoxing impurities in the body), it’s pure quackery. But everyone has different definitions of detoxing or cleansing. For instance, the product she names contains milk thistle and choline. Both ingredients are in the Weight Loss and Liver Support (WLLS) product, which work to help metabolize fat droplets in the liver and subsequently support healing liver tissues and improving energy metabolism. The problem with the product she names, JSHEALTH Detox + Debloat Formula, is that both those ingredients are well under their respective effective dosages. The effective doses are in WLLS at a daily dose of 400 mg of choline and 498 mg of milk thistle, whereas they only have 82 mg of choline and 120 mg of milk thistle in a full daily dose. Further, their turmeric amount is useless. Bottom line: If someone is using this JSHEALTH product to somehow cleanse or detox their body, it is useless. If they are using it to minimize bloating and it somehow works—well, okay. Not sure how, but hey, anything is possible depending on the physiological state of the recipient. Below is a related FAQ from the website.
>
> All articles: https://www.dotfit.com/search.aspx?q=detox
>
> What is the best way to detoxify my body? https://www.dotfit.com/content-35775.html
>
> You don't need to. The notion that fasting cleanses actually “clean” the body is more of a marketing ploy than sound science. The human body naturally expels toxins via detoxification pathways in the gallbladder, liver, kidneys, digestive system, lungs and skin. The gastrointestinal tract contains 70% of the body’s immune response, and one of its major roles is to remove waste from the body. Currently, there is little to no research that waste buildup or constipation (straining to pass hard stools) causes cancer, health issues or disease. Most importantly, follow your Playspan® dietary support program to calorically efficiently meet your protein and vitamin and mineral recommendations. That said, if still compelled to clean up your lifestyle for some period of time, adding any of the following strategies to your lifestyle is easy, healthy and your best bet at “detoxifying” the body: https://www.dotfit.com/content-35775.html.
>
> 1. Minimize added sugars such as soda and sugar-sweetened beverages.
> 2. Eat a diet rich in fresh organic fruits and vegetables. These foods contain fiber, protective phytonutrients, and vitamins and minerals.
> 3. Consume plant oils and unprocessed nuts for anti-inflammatory oils and minerals.
> 4. Eat adequate protein from high-quality sources, fresh eggs, and vegetables. If you eat meat, choose sources that are humanely treated and free of hormones and antibiotics.
> 5. For individuals with allergies, choose foods and supplements that are free of common food allergens such as gluten (from grains) and casein (from dairy).
> 6. Eliminate stimulants, caffeine, alcohol, tobacco products, modified food ingredients, and chemicals, and drink adequate amounts of clean water free of contaminants. For pure water, consider a reverse osmosis filter system or RO water, which can be found at many natural grocery and health food stores.
> 7. Consume adequate fiber—28 to 35 grams a day—to promote proper bowel function.
> 8. Get enough restorative sleep (7.5 to 9 hours per night of sound sleep or stage 4 sleep is optimal).
> 9. **Source answer:**

> FAQ
>
> Q: Can you explain when I would need Creatine Monohydrate versus ExtremeCreatineXXXL+?
> A: Using ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (EC) versus CreatineMonohydrate (CM) is simply based on your goals. If your fitness goal is to improve performance beyond just taking CM, along with your baseline dietary support, such as a multi, omega, and protein as needed, then you would use EC because it contains the same CM but also contains beta-alanine, Careflow (vasodilator) for added muscle endurance and force production, along with glutamine for added exercise recovery.
>
> See below for a related FAQ.
>
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. See below for your answer, along with some other information and related FAQ.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great Monday.
>
> FAQ
>
> Q: What is the difference between the dotFIT ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ and CreatineMonohydrate?
> A: CreatineMonohydrate is simply creatine monohydrate. ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ is the same creatine monohydrate (NSF-CS) but also contains beta-alanine, glutamine, and Carflow™ (vasodilator) for their respective additive performance benefits. For a product description, including a short video, go to the dotFIT website store and click on the product you would like to learn about. Here are the direct links to the two products you requested: CreatineMonohydrate and ExtremeCreatineXXXXL+. Once you click in, go to “Overview” to get product details and video.
>
> More Info
>
> Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ as a Standalone Product
>
> ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ to maintain all until the end of the cycle
>
> On training days, take one (1) scoop before training and one (1) scoop after. You may use it along with your pre- and post-workout shake or meal containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> For users over 150 lb seeking to maximize performance, such as in strength, speed, and power activities, add a third scoop at any other time of the day with a meal or shake.
>
> On non-training days, to maintain Cr, BA, and CF levels, take one (1) scoop with the morning meal or shake and one (1) scoop with the evening meal or shake. If using three (3) scoops, spread each scoop throughout the day with meals or shakes—for example, morning, afternoon, and early evening. True creatine-only supplementation maintenance is 0.045 g/lb/day.
>
> Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ within Size and Performance Stacking Protocols
>
> See complete supplement stacking programs for competitive size and performance athletes here: Creatine Muscle & Performance Stacks.
>
> CreatineMonohydrate as a Standalone Product
>
> Creatine Loading & Maintenance Strategy* (refer to Mix Dosing & Stacking Instructions in Appendix)
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading (true loading 0.14 g/lb/day) & Maintenance* Strategy (true maintenance 0.045 g/lb/day)
>
> Mix 1 scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of your favorite fluid or shake and take it 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal or drink for the first 5 days. Thereafter, take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals or drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> Training days: 1 dose before the workout and 1 after, with meals or drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> On non-training days, take 1 scoop with the AM meal and 1 scoop with the PM meal.
>
> *If under 175 lb, you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half; that is, after the loading period, take a total of 5 g daily and split as described or take it all in 1 dose daily.
