> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. In a nutshell, both ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ (EC) and NO7Rage, when used properly, would most likely contribute to muscle size and growth equally since they contain like ingredients, other than the caffeine in NO7, which has unique performance-enhancing benefits. EC is a non-stimulant, multi-ingredient pre-workout product, while NO7 has the stimulant effect of caffeine along with taurine and glycerol. Both products contain creatine, beta-alanine, and vasodilators (EC uses Careflow, while NO7 uses citrulline malate and Nitrosigine). You can basically take your choice related to muscle hypertrophy goals, but NO7 adds an additional performance edge. That said, EC has glutamine for added recovery and cell volumizing.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> The creatine monohydrate in all our creatine products and flavors (NO7, CreatineMonohydrate, and ExtremeCreatineXXXL+) is the same raw material, thus exactly the same creatine.
>
> When Creapure ran out of raw materials a couple of years ago, we had to rush to find another manufacturer. The one we found was an even cleaner creatine monohydrate, as documented by our NSF-CS testing, which has the strictest allowed levels of creatinine. See an older FAQ below for more information and test differences. All that said, there are probably some fragment statements living in places on our website regarding Creapure from when we used it. Our webmaster will do a search to eliminate them.
>
> Thanks, and have a great day.
>
> Q: Are you still using Creapure as your creatine monohydrate source?
> A: We found a better one, which is why we NSF-CS the product—to prove it is cleaner than any other raw material, including Creapure.
>
> When Creapure ran out of raw materials about 2 years ago, we had to find another supplier, which we did, and the new supplier actually had a purer creatine monohydrate (see test results below).
>
> Now our source of creatine surpasses Creapure specs in creatinine levels, making ours purer. Further, ours passes all NSF-CS standards, meaning it is as clean and pure as it gets, and therefore any argument on raw material quality is moot.
>
> Creatine Monohydrate – min 99.9%
> Creatinine – Creapure: max 100 ppm; Creatine Monohydrate used in dotFIT products is lower, at 50 ppm
> Dicyanamide – max 50 ppm
> Dihydrotriazine – max 3 ppm
> Heavy Metals – max 10 ppm
> CAS No. 6020-87-7
> Same Nutritional Profile
> No Allergens
> Our ingredients in all creatine products are Kosher, Halal, Vegan, Non-GMO, and not on the WADA/NSF/NFL Banned Attributes list.

> FAQ
> Q: Are you still using Creapure as your creatine monohydrate source?
> A: We found a better one, which is why we NSF-CS the product—to prove it is cleaner than any other raw material, including Creapure. Moreover, the new creatine raw material allows us to get many additional certifications (e.g., Kosher, Halal, Vegan, Non-GMO).
>
> When Creapure ran out of raw materials about 3 years ago, we had to find another supplier, which we did, and the new supplier actually had a purer creatine monohydrate (see test results below).
>
> Now our source of creatine surpasses Creapure specs in creatinine levels, making ours slightly purer. This difference is minor, but it’s good for bragging rights. Further, ours passes all NSF-CS standards, meaning it is as clean and pure as it gets, and therefore any argument on raw material quality is moot.
>
> Comparison
> Creatine Monohydrate – min 99.9%
> Creatinine in Creapure: 60–100 ppm; Creatine Monohydrate now used in dotFIT products is lower, at ≤50 ppm
> Dicyandiamide (DCD): not detectable
> Dihydrotriazine: not detectable
> Heavy Metals – max 10 ppm
> CAS No. 6020-87-7
> Same Nutritional Profile
> No Allergens
> Our/dF ingredients in all creatine products are Kosher, Halal, Vegan, Non-GMO, and not on the WADA/NSF/NFL Banned Attributes list.
>

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us, and sorry for the delayed response. All our products are third-party tested after completion, and most also go through the NSF Certified for Sport (NSF-CS) process (a fourth-party test, if you will), which is a significant extra cost per lot and a test that goes beyond standard NSF certification and third-party testing.
>
> Therefore, products that are not in high demand, such as some fat-loss products including LeanMR or Weight Loss & Liver Support (WLLS), are not sent through the additional NSF-CS process for our pro and college teams and other organizations that require NSF-CS (drug-tested athletes), so we can keep these products affordable for everyone.
>
> Again, all products are third-party tested by different outside labs, such as Natural Products Association's GMP certification, Covance, LA Analytical, and Chemical Solutions—click here for all facilities and testing. They are manufactured in NSF-certified facilities and go through the same manufacturing rigor and processes, so the only reason for the additional NSF-CS (fourth-party test, if you will) is for customers who require it.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day.

*thread_date: 2026-04-30 · topics: iron, multivitamin, multivitamin formulation, nutrient absorption, zinc · source: `ac719c1282c922c6` 2026/Design criteria note MVM, iron&zinc together.docx*

- (item-specific) …

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Both iron and zinc are contained in all Practitioner complete multivitamin and mineral formulas (MVM), with exceptions for some people over 50 years for whom extra iron can be a problem, because they are 2 of the 20 known potentially under-consumed nutrients in U.S. diets and because our Practitioner MVMs are in a controlled-release matrix, so all ingredients make it to their target tissues. Further, many of our Practitioners read their clients' biomarkers before and after supplementation and find that all iron- and zinc-dependent enzymes and proteins are improved, validating efficacy. With all due respect, you have probably misinterpreted the studies or simply taken them out of context. There would be a lot of dying humans if they could not absorb all essential nutrition regardless of how it is consumed, since that is the way humans consume foods and mixed meals. Both zinc and iron are essential to life. The human body developed an amazing digestive tract that can present the nutrients from food and supplements in the proper forms to be absorbed into the body to maintain life.
>
> If you would like to learn more about designing the best possible MVM formula that complements Western diets, I have included a section (links below) from the Practitioner's Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) that our Practitioners, including pharmacists and dietitians, use for training and education.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day.
>
> The daily amounts of vitamins and minerals currently known to be in the safe and optimal range are captured in the peer-reviewed Practitioner's Dietary Supplement Reference Guide (PDSRG) and contained within those documented amounts in the dotFIT formulas—the design of the Multivitamin and Mineral Formula (MVM).

> Bloodwork:
>
> At the end of the day, overall, your bloodwork looks good in the most important areas, particularly cardiovascular/metabolic health: A1C is normal at 5.4%, triglycerides 41, LDL 63, ApoB 50, hs-CRP 0.6, and kidney function is normal (creatinine 0.9, eGFR 93). Vitamin D is high-normal at 91 ng/mL (might go lower on supplementing—if you are). The findings that might deserve follow-up are potassium at 5.9, which is high enough that it should be confirmed (sometimes it may be from the blood draw, but true hyperkalemia shouldn't be ignored); elevated mean corpuscular volume (MCV) of 105, indicating macrocytosis (large red blood cells), which may be related to your B12/folate status; and low total protein/globulin. All in all, things look good based on a favorable metabolic and cardiovascular profile. Maybe the few abnormalities deserve follow-up—most importantly, confirming the elevated potassium and investigating the elevated MCV.
>
> Keep on Trucking!

> - Take ½–1 scoop immediately following the workout.
> - Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10 minutes before the workout and consume at a pace so it’s finished at the end of activity.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. Your protein needs are simple: Make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily. If you are using AminoFormula (AF) for exercise recovery, the most you can count it toward your protein goal, no matter how much you use, is 40 g (2 scoops split). See below for proper use within your baseline dietary support program and exercise. Below that is a related Q&A. All that said, you can also use AF to fortify a low-protein meal/diet/foods such as plant-based diets, low-protein breakfast, etc., to raise the meal’s protein content with caloric efficiency or break a fast.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day.
>
> AminoFormula in Baseline to Control Body Composition – No Stacking
>
> General Health, Recovery, Weight Control and Performance Supplement Program with AminoFormula
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
>
> - dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 yrs or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 yrs if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 yrs use Kids; children 12–17 yrs use 1-Active; or use the Alln1 SuperBlend™.
> - Take as directed with meals.
> - WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix, based on caloric allotment for goal.
> - Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 gm of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
> - Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> As needed:
>
> - Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content, including dotFIT protein shakes).*
> - Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
> - Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
> - Super Omega 3.
> - Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> *All food labels, including protein powders, must contain calcium levels by serving, so always check.
>
> Workout Days:
>
> AminoFormula (AF) – count as 25–40 g protein.
>
> - Take ½–1 scoop ~10 minutes before and may continue to drink during the first 20 minutes of the workout (or to midpoint).
> - Take ½–1 scoop immediately following the workout.
> - Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10 minutes before the workout and consume at a pace so it’s finished at the end of the activity.
>
> Q: There is no protein listed on AminoFormula (AF); can I count it as a protein?
>
> A: You may add 25–40 g (1–2 scoops) of protein to your daily protein intake when using AF as directed, but generally no more regardless of daily intake. The reason AminoFormula (AF) does not publish a protein amount is because it is purposely not a complete protein, as it contains only the essential amino acids that are directly responsible for protein synthesis activation in proportion to the demand, and therefore serves as a low-calorie, fast-acting, high-anabolic-signaling product for activating muscle protein synthesis (MPS) beyond intact protein itself.
>
> Your total food/shake protein intake throughout the day supplies the remaining amino acids (and they can be made in the body) necessary for the complete and prolonged synthesis. That said, AF can be used to fortify a low-protein meal/food (e.g., plant-based, low-protein breakfast, etc.) to raise the total food protein biological value (BV)—i.e., eliminate the AA limiting factor for MPS indigenous to weaker protein sources. Further, AF is vegan-friendly.
>
> More info:
>
> AF serves to enhance the MPS process beyond protein alone or serve as a low-calorie, high-anabolic extra source of protein. A protein equivalent in 1 scoop of AF is generally counted as ~25 g, as it takes ~25–35 g of a complete protein to get the amount of the EAA found in one serving of AF, including the 6 g of BCAA. However, regardless of your total AF intake, we advise you only add it as 25–40 g of protein to your 1 gm/LB/lean body mass (LBM)/day of protein needs. Mindful that active strength/performance athletes still maintain the 1 gm/LB/LBM/D of whole/intact protein sources because different whole food proteins have other helpful properties/nutrients, including satiety.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. What you are experiencing is what many people go through after dieting to reach a lower body fat. They reach it and celebrate, and then find themselves having an ongoing difficult relationship with food. The more aggressive the weight loss (continuous calorie restrictions leading to >2 lbs/week), the more common this relationship with food becomes. The primary reason for successful weight loss is the trigger event—i.e., what motivated you in the first place, such as the wedding in your case. You need to find another motivating factor for the rest of your life and then follow the weekly average calorie intake that keeps you at the goal weight/body fat. This means you can incorporate the foods you like (on weekends or whatever day) within those weekly calories. Example: if your maintenance calories are ~2,500 calories/day to maintain your desired body fat after you have reached your goal, you can consume an average of 2,200 calories/day during the week, leaving you 5 days × 300 calories = 1,500 calories extra for favorite foods on top of the normal calorie intake. And remember, you can always move more (walking, playing sports, biking, etc.) to offset the extra calorie intake, especially on the weekends.
>
> The majority of us former athletes whose sport required regular aggressive dieting (e.g., bodybuilding, wrestling, jockeys, combat sports, etc.) have ongoing problematic relationships with food (we call it diet PTSD) that we control based on lifelong fitness goals. For me, a retired bodybuilder and now fitness/nutrition leader, I still make a living by being in shape. And you too will be able to control your overall calorie intake if you had to “support your family” by doing so.
>
> So find your motivation, log your foods for at least 90 days, lose no more than 1 lb/week until you reach your goal, at which time your calories will be adjusted to your maintenance. Assuming you are not a member of one of our licensed facilities, you can use the dotFIT program to accomplish all this. It contains the diet information you need to get started and stay on track with your caloric intake/menu planning, etc. It will also give a dietary support program (see below) to help you stick to the plan and reach your goal healthily. I’m also connecting you here with [NAME] (certified counselor and cc’d here) so, as necessary, [NAME] can get you pointed in the right direction, including proper access to the tools you need. And finally, at the bottom of the email, you will see some tips and tricks you may find helpful.
>
> Hope this all helps!
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> ThermAccel in Baseline
>
> Links will take you to a brief product description including video
>
> Daily:
> dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 years or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 years if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 years use Kids; children 12–17 years use 1-Active.
>
> Take as directed with meals.
>
> ThermAccel (TA)
>
> Take 2 tablets at breakfast and 2 with lunch, accompanied by at least 8 oz of fluids. Individuals sensitive to caffeine should begin with 1 tablet twice daily to assess tolerance before increasing to the maximum dose of 4 tablets daily. Do not use TA with other stimulants or by individuals taking heart medications. Once fat/weight loss goals are achieved and lifestyle changes are firmly in place, ThermAccel can be discontinued.
>
> Daily as needed:
> Favorite dotFIT protein mix
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 g of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> If exercising, use as directed to get ~25–35 g of protein 30–40 minutes before workout and repeat dose immediately post-workout (see below).
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>
> SuperCalcium (read all food and shake labels)
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal; males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> SuperOmega-3
>
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes.
>
> Workout days
>
> dotFIT Protein of choice*. Use as directed to get:
>
> 25–35 g of protein 30–40 minutes before workout and repeat the same dose immediately following exercise.
>
> *May substitute favorite dotFIT Nutrition High Protein Bar based on venue convenience.
>
> Here are your natural options that have been highly successful in curbing late-night appetites, including the proverbial sweet tooth:
>
> Combine Weight Loss and LiverSupport (WLLS) with ThermAccel and take daily as directed.
>
> Use the Alln1 SuperBlend™ instead of your current multivitamin and mineral formula (and any other supplements it replaces) and take 1 scoop in the AM and 1 in the PM to cut the late-night appetite.
>
> The most popular way of incorporating Alln1 SuperBlend™ into a lifestyle is 1 scoop combined with 1 serving of your favorite vanilla protein, fruit, ice, and water. Take the other dose as desired (often taken with water/favorite fluid and ice in the evening to help control appetite; may add ½–1 scoop AminoFormula if needed).
>
> Incorporate bodybuilder tricks at night:
>
> Brush your teeth after your last meal (sends a signal to the brain you’re done).
>
> Chew sugarless gum as your dessert.
>
> Drink a diet soda for dessert.
>
> Mix AminoFormula with a low-calorie beverage and drink it for your dessert.
>
> Save your last calories for a low-calorie dessert such as low-calorie frozen yogurt or a protein bar.

> DotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender. If 50 yrs or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 yrs if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 yrs use Kids; children 12–17 yrs use 1-Active. — OR Alln1 SuperBlendTM.

> Creatine Monohydrate Loading (true loading = .14 g/lb/day) & Maintenance (true maintenance .045 g/lb/day)* protocol:
>
> Mix 1 scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first 5 days. > Training days: 1 dose before workout and 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> Non-training days: take 1 dose with the AM meal and 1 dose with the PM meal.
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> All-Natural WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix (based on caloric allotment for goal).
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get ~1 g of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein and calorie needs.
>
> Super Omega 3:
>
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> Super Calcium (read all food labels for calcium content):
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000–1200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals; if you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal. Males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> Workout days:
>
> All-Natural WheySmooth or other favorite dotFIT protein mix (based on caloric allotment for goal).
>
> Take as directed (~25–35 g protein) 30–40 minutes before workout and repeat immediately post-exercise.
>
> Creatine Monohydrate:
>
> On training days, use one dose before workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> *When not dosing by body weight and under 175 lbs, you may cut the daily maintenance dose in half—i.e., after the loading period, take a total of 5 g daily and split as described.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. First, your desired deficit of 1,000 cal/day is not a good idea if you want to preserve/build muscle while losing body fat. Manage the deficit to average approximately 500 calories/day. Adjust your calories in and/or out as needed to maintain this average (see below for monitoring and adjusting for continuous body fat reduction). Make sure you monitor your body fat with your weight because, with the programs we deliver, people put on muscle while losing body fat, so body weight alone does not tell the true body composition change story. We also added CreatineMonohydrate to your baseline supplement program below since you are an experienced exerciser.
>
> The dotFIT program contains the diet and self-monitoring information you need to get started and stay on track with your caloric intake/menu planning, etc., as you pursue your body composition/fitness goals.
>
> Assuming you are not a member of one of our licensed facilities, I’m connecting you here with [NAME] (certified counselor and cc’d here) so [NAME] can get you pointed in the right direction, including proper access to the tools you need. Below is the baseline dietary support program (added CreatineMonohydrate) for all active people who want to remain active that forms the foundation for health, daily recovery, and exercise-induced gains so that you can protect and continue to build lean body mass (LBM). After using the baseline supplement program, if you want to take your fitness goals further, you can use our supplement screening tool to get the proper recommendations or simply contact [NAME].
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day. We will be with you all the way on your fitness journey.
>
> Level 1: Creatine in Baseline for Optimizing Body Composition with Health & Performance
>
> Click on product links for a brief product description, including a short video.
>
> Daily:
>
> DotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender: use 2-Active, OR Alln1 SuperBlendTM
>
> Take as directed with meals.
>
> Daily as needed:
>
> WheySmooth
>
> Use as directed to make sure you get approximately 1 gram of protein per pound of LBM (or weight if not overweight) from all sources, including foods, divided 3–5 times daily.
>
> Can be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired supporting contents) to shore up total daily protein and calorie needs.
>
> Omega 3
>
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish. Take 1 if consuming more than 8 oz/week but less than 16 oz/week; take 2 if consuming 8 oz/week or less. It is not necessary if consuming 16 oz/week or more unless for athletic recovery purposes; then increase as directed.
>
> Calcium Complex (read all food labels for calcium content)
>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (approximately 1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Females take 1 or 2 daily with meals. If you need to take 2, take one with the AM meal and the second with the PM meal. Males take 1 only if necessary.
>
> 2nd Week Start: CreatineMonohydrate (CrM)*
>
> CreatineMonohydrate loading (true loading = 0.14 g/lb/day) and maintenance (true maintenance = 0.045 g/lb [LBM]/day)* protocol.
>
> Mix 1 scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of a favorite fluid/shake and take 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal/drink for the first 5 days. Thereafter, take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals/drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> Training days: Take 1 dose before the workout and 1 after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> Non-training days: Take 1 dose with the AM meal and 1 dose with the PM meal.
>
> Workout days
>
> WheySmooth
>
> Take as directed (25–35 g protein) 30–40 minutes before the workout and repeat immediately post-exercise.
>
> Creatine Monohydrate
>
> On training days, use one dose before the workout and one after with meals/drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> *When not dosing by body weight and under 175 lbs, you may cut the daily maintenance dose in half; i.e., after the loading period, take a total of 5 g daily and split as described.
>
> Monitoring and Adjustments:
>
> Measurable or visual reduction in body fat and/or weight proceeds in a consistent manner, e.g., a decrease in circumference inches and/or the desired average decrease in body fat per week.
>
> If progress stops or slows dramatically, use one or a combination of adjustments to restart the process:
>
> • Increase daily activities, such as daily steps or other non-athletic/exercise activities.
> • Standing and pacing burns 1.5–2 times more calories than sitting for the same period.
> • There are approximately 2,000–2,500 steps (depending on stride length) in a mile. Walking 2,000 steps will burn approximately 75–150 more calories (depending on individual size) than sitting for the same time, takes approximately 20–30 minutes, and can be done anywhere, even in the office, while on the phone, or watching TV.
> • Increase cardio workout time and/or intensity and include interval training if necessary.
> • Example: If doing 30 minutes 5 days/week, go to 45 minutes and add interval work within the time frame.
> • Decrease food intake approximately 200–300 calories per day or remove a small portion (carbs/fats) from the largest meal other than the pre-workout meal (incorporate 3 days low/1 high as necessary).
> • Maximize dietary support beyond baseline as necessary (muscle building, thermogenic/energy partitioning, etc.).
>
> Repeat the process any time body fat is stable for at least one week. Always remember: if you are supplementing properly and you stop losing fat, you need to eat less, move/burn more, or use a combination of the two, regardless of what you read or hear from others. As you lower calorie intake, make sure your protein intake stays at or above 1 gram per pound of body weight to protect LBM.

Take as much time as necessary to reach this goal—for example, if you are at 5K now, try to add an extra 1K every couple of weeks and make sure that a portion of your steps are done briskly. If you are doing regular higher-intensity cardiovascular activity than walking, it’s not necessary to perform any of your daily steps at a brisk pace.

>

If 50 years or older, use Over50 unless noted below; if female under 50, use Women’s; males and very active females (competitive training protocols) under 50 use 2-Active, with both genders continuing use until 65 years if still training competitively, at which time every person will use Over50; children under 12 years use Kids; children 12–17 years use 1-Active. Or Alln1 SuperBlend™.

>
> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1,000–1,200 mg) from food/shake intake.

> Super Omega 3.

>

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. All 3 Creatine products contain creatine monohydrate, the same creatine that is the only ingredient in the dotFIT CreatineMonohydrate. ExtremeCreatineXXXL and NO7 PREWORKOUT contain added pre-workout ingredients such as beta-alanine, caffeine, vasodilators, etc. The products are often combined, especially CreatineMonohydrate and ExtremeCreatineXXXL (non-stimulant pre-workout). See below for related information, including how to combine the different products.
>
> Thanks again and have a great day.
>
> FAQ
>
> Q: What is the difference between the dotFIT ExtremeCreatineXXXL and CreatineMonohydrate?
> A: CreatineMonohydrate is simply creatine monohydrate. ExtremeCreatineXXXL is the same creatine monohydrate (NSF-CS) but also contains beta-alanine, glutamine, and Carflow™ (vasodilator) for their respective additive performance benefits. For a product description, including a short video, go to the dotFIT website store and click on the product you would like to learn about. Here are the direct links to the 2 products you requested: CreatineMonohydrate and ExtremeCreatineXXXXL+. Once you click in, go to “Overview” to get product details and video.
>
> More Info
>
> Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ as a Standalone Product
>
> ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ to maintain all until the end of the cycle
>
> On training days, take one (1) scoop before training and one (1) scoop after. You may use it along with your pre- and post-workout shake or meal containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> For those users >150 LBS and seeking to maximize performance, such as strength, speed, and power activities, add a third scoop any other time of the day with a meal or shake.
>
> On non-training days, to maintain Cr, BA, and CF levels, take one (1) scoop with the morning meal or shake and one (1) scoop with the evening meal or shake. If using three (3) scoops, spread each scoop throughout the day with meals or shakes—that is, morning, afternoon, and early evening. True creatine-only supplementation maintenance is .045 g/lb/d.
>
> Dosing ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ within Size and Performance Stacking Protocols
>
> See complete supplement stacking programs for competitive size and performance athletes here: Creatine Muscle & Performance Stacks.
>
> CreatineMonohydrate as a standalone product
>
> Creatine Loading & Maintenance Strategy* (Refer to Mix Dosing & Stacking Instructions in Appendix)
>
> CreatineMonohydrate Loading (true loading .14 g/lb/d) & Maintenance* Strategy (true maintenance .045 g/lb/d)
>
> Mix 1 scoop (5 g) with 4–8 oz of your favorite fluid or shake and take it 4 times daily with a protein- and/or carbohydrate-containing meal or drink for the first 5 days. Thereafter, take 1 scoop twice daily to maintain stores. To help maximize creatine uptake, split doses throughout the day with meals or drinks containing protein and/or carbohydrate.
>
> Training days: 1 dose before the workout and 1 after with meals or drinks. May mix with your pre- and post-training formula.
>
> On non-training days, take 1 scoop with the AM meal and 1 scoop with the PM meal.
>
> *If under 175 LBS, you can cut the daily maintenance dose in half—that is, after the loading period, take a total of 5 g daily and split as described or take it all in 1 dose daily.
>
> Q: I wanted to double-check and see if NO7 Rage3 and Extreme Creatine formulas can both be used together since they both have creatine in them?
> A: You can use the NO7 PREWORKOUT (formerly Rage) along with ExtremeCreatineXXXL (EC), and this is common. *The goal when using NO7 and EC together is to maintain 5 g creatine daily between the 2 products if under 175 LBS, and 7.5–10 g of creatine daily if over 175 LBS. It does not hurt to go over, but it is more economical for you to add the daily amounts when using both products. See below for best use when using both products in the baseline dietary support program.
>
> NO7 PREWORKOUT (NO) & ExtremeCreatineXXXL (EC) Performance & Size Supplement Plan in Baseline
>
> ActiveMV - Multivitamin & Mineral Formula
>
> Take two (2) daily: one (1) immediately following the first large meal and one (1) after the final meal of the day. Or use Alln1 SuperBlend™.
>
> SuperOmega-3
>
> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16 oz/week; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.
>
> FirstString (or WheySmooth, depending on daily calorie allotment—take as directed)
>
> Take two (2) scoops 30–45 minutes before the workout, or use your favorite dotFIT® bar for convenience.
>
> Take two (2) scoops after the workout.
>
> Use anytime throughout the day as a supplement to a meal or by itself, mixed with desired ingredients to add protein or calories as needed to meet daily requirements.
>
> Be sure to ingest approximately one (1) g of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, including foods, divided 4–5 times daily along with the pre- and post-workout shakes. The pre- and post-workout shakes combined generally supply 40–50 g of the daily requirement.
>
> ExtremeCreatineXXXL+ to maintain all until the end of the cycle*
>
> On training days, take 1 scoop after training (NO7 has the necessary pre-training ingredients). You may use it along with your post-workout shake or meal containing protein and/or carbohydrate. For users >150 LBS seeking to maximize performance such as strength, speed, and power activities, add a second scoop any other time of the day with a meal or shake.
>
> On non-training days, to maintain Cr, BA, and Carflow levels, take 1 scoop with the AM meal or shake and 1 scoop with the PM meal or shake. If using three (3) scoops, spread each scoop throughout the day with meals or shakes—that is, morning, afternoon, and early evening. True creatine-only maintenance is .045 g/lb/d.
>
> NO7 PREWORKOUT (workout days only because EC daily maintains Cr & BA saturation)
>
> Take 1.5–2.5 scoops depending on weight (if caffeine-sensitive, start with a lower dose and increase to the proper weight dose if not uncomfortably affected by caffeine) approximately 10 minutes before the workout. See directions on the label for weight dosages.
>
> NO7 PREWORKOUT contains 150 mg of caffeine per scoop. As a reference, Starbucks Grande drip coffee contains approximately 330 mg of caffeine, close to the same amount as 2 scoops of NO7 Rage.
>
> *The goal when using NO7 and EC together is to maintain 5 g creatine daily between the 2 products if under 175 LBS, and 7.5–10 g of creatine daily if over 175 LBS.

>
> Sorry about the diagnosis, but if you are diligent in managing your insulin, everything is good. We did not see a specific question about what you would want us to answer. As far as your diet goes, I’m sure you got instructions from your attending physician. Although a higher-protein, lower-carb diet is common in diabetes, most healthy diets are on the table, as long as you are managing your insulin properly. Ideally, try to get protein and fiber at every meal, and use whole grains rather than refined grains. To gain muscle, you need to keep your calories above maintenance with protein at ~1 gm/pound of lean body mass (LBM)/D, and you can add CreatineMonohydrate and AminoFormula for dietary support along with your complete multivitamin and mineral formula (or Alln1 SuperBlend™), SuperOmega-3 as needed.

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Always great to see you—albeit on camera 😊. The short answer is that the vast majority of dotFIT clients would rarely need a separate magnesium supplement if they are following their proper baseline supplementation and adding other dotFIT products as needed. And yes, the Tolerable Upper Limit Levels (UL) of magnesium is only 350 mg. But as you mentioned, that only means your supplemental magnesium should supposedly not exceed that number. However, spreading it out like you are, it is fine to go slightly over that UL (see below for more info from our Sleep Aid formula lectures). I also place a related Q&A below that might be helpful.
>
> Hope this helps—and have a great evening.
> Reach out anytime!
>
> Esquivel et al. 2024—Current Evidence on Common Dietary Supplements for Sleep Quality
>
> With regards to sleep, Magnesium (MG) has several essential functions that include: GABA activation (calming neurotransmitter), increases in melatonin production, muscle relaxation, regulation of stress responses, regulation of calcium levels which promote muscle contraction and relaxation, and anti-anxiety effects. Furthermore, low magnesium status has been associated with chronic inflammatory stress in both animal and human research studies. However, despite its popularity, there are few clinical benefits to MG supplementation (MGS) in individuals with adequate serum magnesium. There is a potential for MGS in individuals with low serum magnesium to improve sleep quality.
>
> Our world (dotFIT) should not be MG deficient/insufficient; therefore, the 100 mg added from Sleep Aid is safe and appropriate.*
>
> 2 Active: 200 mg
> 1 Woman’s: 100 mg (because most need Ca supp)
> 2 Over50: 150 mg
> 1 svg SuperBlend: 160 mg
> 1 SuperCalcium: 125 mg
> 2 Sleep Aid: 100 mg
>
> *UL of 350 mg from supplements is generally disregarded because it’s based on a single dose, not spread out as ours would be. Further, the Ca/Mg ratio should be protected (2–3:1).
>
> Final conclusion/takeaway: Meet ~400–500 mg/day through diet and supplement intake that includes a complete MVM containing MG and the other ~20 known under-consumed VMs: dotFIT MVM/Alln1 SuperBlend™ = 100–200 mg; Sleep Aid = 100 mg; diet = 250 mg; daily total is ~450–550 mg MG (2–300 mg in supplements), which would maximize MG’s basic role in sleep quality mechanisms, making high-dose separate MGS unnecessary and likely ineffective.
>
> MG unique contribution: Achieving proper MG intake as described here offers a unique contribution to the enhancement of all the Sleep Aid ingredients’ mechanisms of action related to stress relief, mood, sleep quality, and overall health, since MG is an essential mineral active in these myriad pathways and, when shorted, all systems suffer.
>
> Q: Will you all sell a magnesium supplement? Also, if my doctor is prescribing that I take one, are there any legitimate options? Not sure if you can answer that for me, but thank you for your time.
>
> A: Thanks for contacting us. We will not be making a separate magnesium supplement any time soon, and only because we do not have a large enough market in our distribution channel (sport and fitness and Practitioner channels) to support the large product runs necessary to be cost-effective. And also because we have magnesium in all our multivitamin and mineral formulas (MVM), Alln1 SuperBlend™, SuperCalcium, and some other products. Meaning, for the majority of our users, when combining diet and supplements, they would not need more magnesium, or they would be over the Tolerable Upper Limit Levels (UL). Of course, there may be exceptions, such as yourself, where a doctor has prescribed additional magnesium for something they diagnosed.
>
> When searching for a separate magnesium supplement, look to the NSF website or another third-party certification.
>
> [NAME] [NAME]

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The Alln1 SuperBlend™ (SB) contains all you need for a multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) and contains a plant omega-3, which is different than the marine omega-3 you are currently using. The SB, of course, contains many other health-promoting nutrients, as it includes a full day's recommendation for vitamins, minerals, antioxidants, probiotics, prebiotics, ashwagandha, turmeric and mushroom blend, along with 6 g of fiber and 2 full servings of vegetables.
>
> Calcium and creatine are not in the SB. Below are related FAQs that cover proper usage with dotFIT supplements.
>
> Thanks again, and have a great day.
>
> FAQs and more information
>
> Q: If I order the Alln1 Super Blend, does that replace the MV over 50, Super Omega and Super Calcium?
> A: You do not need to take your Over50 multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) when using the Alln1 SuperBlend™ (SB). Although the SB contains omega-3s, it is plant source, thus acts slightly differently in the body than a marine source, such as the SuperOmega-3. Therefore, you can continue using your SuperOmega if you do not consume fatty fish regularly and your SuperCalcium if not getting 1200 mg/day from diet.
>
> Q: If someone is using the dotFIT health supplements and wants to switch to the Alln1 SuperBlend™ (SB), what will they no longer need?
> A: They will no longer need the Multivitamin, SuperiorAntioxidant, UltraProbiotic, and Digestive Enzymes because those formulas are in the SB. They may add these additional dF health products as needed: SuperOmega (SB has a plant Omega-3—see below FAQ) and extra V-D (SB contains 1000 IUs, but sometimes more is needed to reach >30 ng/ml). If recommended, you can add BrainHealth, JointSkinCollagen+, and SuperCalcium, as these are purposely not in the SB because not everyone of all ages would necessarily need these. All other dotFIT products (e.g., performance, body fat/weight loss, proteins, bars, etc.) can be added as usual.
>
> Q: I am taking the Alln1 SuperBlend™ (SB) daily. Is there any need for me to keep taking the SuperOmega-3 fish oil?
> A: If you are not eating at least 2 svgs/week of fatty fish, then yes, you should continue to take the dotFIT SuperOmega-3 because it is marine Omega-3, which has complementary actions to vegan/plant omega-3 sources. Although the SB contains omega-3s, it is from flaxseed to keep the product vegan-friendly for pure vegan clients, giving others the option of adding a marine source. Again, for basic health protection, if you are eating ≥8 oz of fish weekly, you do not need to add the SuperOmega-3, as you will have a healthy dose of all omega-3 sources. For extended athletic purposes (e.g., recovery, performance, body composition, etc.), the dosage of a marine source (SuperOmega-3) is generally 3–6 g/day, which would likely require additional supplementation based on fish intake (e.g., if eating less than 8 oz/week and using SB, add 3–4 SuperOmega-3s).

**Source answer:**

> Hi [NAME],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. As long as your client does not have a clinical issue, such as gastroparesis (stomach paralysis) and/or bowel restriction (both potential side effects in GLP drug users, in which their medical provider should be aware or confirm), then making sure they get enough fiber (work up to ~25 g/day) and micronutrients using smoothies (Alln1 SuperBlend™ and LeanMeal) as necessary and staying hydrated will certainly help. See below for more information.
>
> Thanks again and have a great evening.
>
> More info:
>
> Constipation is one of the most common non-serious side effects in people using GLP-1 receptor agonists (such as semaglutide or tirzepatide). It usually occurs because these drugs slow gastric emptying and overall intestinal motility, reduce appetite (so total food volume drops), and many users unintentionally reduce fiber, fluid, and magnesium intake while dieting. If the issue is not clinical gastroparesis, the goal is simply to restore normal stool volume, hydration, and gut motility.
>
> Practical Recommendation
>
> For most GLP-1 users with mild constipation:
> - Make sure they are taking a complete multivitamin and mineral formula (MVM) and enough protein (1 g/pound/LBM/D).
> - Drink 3 L water daily.
> - Add 5–10 g psyllium fiber.
> - Take 200–400 mg magnesium at night.
> - Ensure 20–30 g fiber/day from foods.
> - Walk after meals.
>
> This simple protocol resolves the issue for the majority of users.
>
> Constipation is one of the most common non-serious side effects in people using GLP-1 receptor agonists (such as semaglutide or tirzepatide). It usually occurs because these drugs slow gastric emptying and overall intestinal motility, reduce appetite (so total food volume drops), and many users unintentionally reduce fiber, fluid, and magnesium intake while dieting. If the issue is not clinical gastroparesis, the goal is simply to restore normal stool volume, hydration, and gut motility.
>
> Below is a practical evidence-based stepwise approach that trainers, clinicians, or users can follow.
>
> 1. Hydration First (Most Common Missing Piece)
>
> Many GLP-1 users dramatically reduce food intake and forget to increase fluids.
>
> Recommendation:
> - 2.5–3.5 L fluid/day.
> - Add electrolytes or a pinch of sodium to improve absorption.
> - Drink 1–2 glasses of water immediately upon waking.
>
> Adequate hydration softens stool and helps fiber work properly.
>
> 2. Add Soluble Fiber (Primary Non-Drug Strategy)
>
> GLP-1 diets often become low-fiber because people eat less food overall.
>
> Best evidence-based fibers:
> - Psyllium husk: 5–10 g/day.
> - Partially hydrolyzed guar gum (PHGG): 5–6 g/day.
> - Inulin or resistant starch: 3–8 g/day.
>
> Benefits:
> - Increases stool bulk.
> - Improves gut microbiome.
> - Supports metabolic health.
>
> Important: fiber must be combined with water.
>
> 3. Magnesium for Motility
>
> Many Americans already have suboptimal magnesium intake, and magnesium can help draw water into the colon.
>
> Common options:
> - Magnesium citrate: 200–400 mg at night.
> - Magnesium glycinate: 200–400 mg/day (gentler but less laxative).
> - Magnesium oxide: stronger laxative effect.
>
> Magnesium is often the simplest effective solution.
>
> 4. Maintain Adequate Food Volume
>
> GLP-1 users sometimes eat so little that there simply isn’t enough bulk to stimulate bowel movements.
>
> Encourage daily intake of:
> - Vegetables.
> - Fruit.
> - Whole grains or resistant starch.
> - Protein foods.
>
> Aiming for 20–30 g fiber/day total is a good target.
>
> 5. Gut Motility Support (Optional)
>
> Certain foods and supplements can help stimulate intestinal movement.
>
> Helpful options:
> - Kiwi fruit (2/day) – strong evidence for constipation relief.
> - Prunes or prune juice.
> - Coffee – stimulates colon motility.
> - Light physical activity after meals.
>
> 6. Probiotics / Prebiotics
>
> GLP-1 therapy can alter the microbiome.
>
> Evidence-supported strains for constipation:
> - Bifidobacterium lactis.
> - Lactobacillus casei.
>
> These may improve stool frequency and consistency.
>
> 7. If Needed: Gentle OTC Options
>
> Occasional use if lifestyle measures are insufficient:
> - Polyethylene glycol (Miralax).
> - Docusate sodium (stool softener).
>
> These are generally safe but should not replace diet and hydration strategies.
>
> Warning Signs (Seek Medical Evaluation)
>
> Constipation with GLP-1 medications should be evaluated if accompanied by:
> - Severe abdominal pain.
> - Persistent vomiting.
> - Inability to pass stool or gas.
> - Signs of gastroparesis.
>
> ✅ Practical “Trainer-Level” Recommendation
>
> For most GLP-1 users with mild constipation:
> - Drink 3 L water daily.
> - Add 5–10 g psyllium fiber.
> - Take 200–400 mg magnesium at night.
> - Ensure 20–30 g fiber/day from foods.
> - Walk after meals.
>
> This simple protocol resolves the issue for the majority of users.

**Source answer:**

> The best carbohydrates after a workout are the ones that rapidly restore muscle glycogen, improve recovery, and support muscle protein synthesis (MPS)—especially when combined with high-quality protein. The “best” choice depends on the goal, training volume, body composition goals, and how quickly another workout is coming.
>
> Best Post-Workout Carbohydrates for Glycogen Replenishment
>
> Fast-Digesting / High-Glycemic Carbohydrates
>
> These are ideal immediately after intense training because they digest quickly and raise insulin efficiently to drive glucose back into muscle.
>
> - White rice
> - Potatoes (especially white or mashed potatoes)
> - Cream of rice
> - Rice cakes
> - Fruit (banana, pineapple, mango, raisins)
> - Honey
> - Sports drinks or glucose polymers
> - Bagels or white bread
> - Oatmeal (moderate-fast depending on type)
> - Low-fat breakfast cereals
>
> These are especially useful for:
>
> - Athletes training multiple times per day
> - High-volume bodybuilding or performance training
> - Glycogen-depleting endurance or interval work
> - Individuals struggling to recover or gain muscle
>
> Best Scientific Combination
>
> The evidence consistently shows that combining carbohydrates with protein is superior to carbs alone for recovery.
>
> A highly effective post-workout target is approximately:
>
> - 0.5–0.75 g carbohydrate per lb bodyweight
> - Plus 25–40 g high-quality protein
>
> Example for a 180-lb exerciser:
>
> - Approximately 90–135 g carbs
> - 30–40 g whey protein
>
> This combination:
>
> - Refills glycogen faster
> - Enhances insulin response
> - Reduces muscle breakdown
> - Improves net muscle protein balance
> - Accelerates recovery
>
> Best Carb Types Based on Goals
>
> | Goal | Best Carb Strategy |
> |---|---|
> | Max muscle gain/performance | Fast carbs + whey protein |
> | Fat loss while preserving muscle | Moderate carbs + high protein |
> | Endurance recovery | Higher total carbs + sodium/electrolytes |
> | Healthy aging/longevity | Moderate clean carbs + protein blend |
> | GLP-1 users | Easily digestible carbs + protein due to appetite suppression |
>
> Real-World Practical Examples
>
> Muscle Gain / Performance
>
> - Whey protein + cream of rice + banana
> - Chocolate milk + rice cakes
> - FirstString™-style carb/protein blend
>
> Fat Loss / Lean Recovery
>
> - WheySmooth™ + fruit
> - LeanMeal™
> - Greek yogurt + berries + oats
>
> Rapid Recovery Between Sessions
>
> - Sports drink + whey isolate
> - White rice + lean protein
> - Potato + protein shake
>
> Important Takeaway for Trainers
>
> The best post-workout carbohydrate is not just about “sugar”—it’s about delivering glucose back into muscle efficiently while pairing it with sufficient amino acids to maximize recovery and muscle protein synthesis. Fast-digesting carbs work best when the goal is performance and recovery, while moderate, fiber-containing carbs may work better when appetite control and body composition are priorities.
>
> Simple Trainer Rule:
>
> “After training, feed the muscle fast: quality carbs refill glycogen, quality protein rebuilds muscle.”
>
> Hey [NAME]! Hope all is well with you guys. You previously provided me with great advice for my early morning CrossFit workouts by mixing EAAs with Gatorade. They have helped tremendously. However now I have to ask, what is best for quick carbs post workout?
>
> Sent from Yahoo Mail for iPhone
>
> On Monday, April 27, 2026, 6:25 PM, [NAME] <[EMAIL]> wrote:
>
> Great!! Thanks again for the great info
>
> Sent from Yahoo Mail for iPhone
>
> On Monday, April 27, 2026, 4:29 PM, [NAME] <[EMAIL]> wrote:
>
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Below is the graph that depicts the sample diet adjustments for bodybuilders, which includes the 3 days low and 1 high from our Practitioner lectures. And yes, you can use the 3 days low and 1 high routine when you know you will be consuming calories over your maintenance. For example, if your maintenance is approximately 3,000 calories per day, consume 2,000 calories for 3 days, which would give you an extra 3,000 calories on your overfeeding day and still keep you on target. Mindful, you can also move more.
>
> Thanks and have a great day
>
> Man, it's great to hear from you! And I'm pumped the EAA/Gatorade combo has been working for you—that's exactly what it should do for those early morning CrossFit sessions.
>
> Okay, so post-workout carbs. Here's the thing—this is actually one of the most critical windows in your entire training day, and most people either skip it or get it wrong.
>
> The bottom line is this: if you don't get fast-acting carbs in immediately after your workout, you will not replenish your glycogen stores before your next training session. That's not opinion, that's just the physiology.
>
> What works best:
>
> 1. Maltodextrin is your go-to fast-acting carb source post-workout. It exits the gastric system fast and gets into your bloodstream quickly—that speed matters here.
> 2. Gatorade—yes, the same stuff you're already using—works for this purpose too because it uses fast-digesting sugars and maltodextrin to get carbs into your system quickly.
> 3. A carb-to-protein ratio of roughly 2:1 is your target post-workout. So if you're getting 25 g of protein, you want around 50 g of carbs alongside it.
> 4. Timing matters—start the process immediately after training, then continue to feed carbs each hour after until your stores are topped off.
>
> For CrossFit specifically, you're doing high-intensity work that hammers glycogen hard. You need those stores refilled before you go back in tomorrow morning.
>
> The dotFIT FirstString is actually a solid option here because it's built with quality protein and fast-acting carbs together—it's designed exactly for pre/post-workout use and hits that carb-to-protein ratio in a convenient package.
>
> And look—since you're clearly dialed in on your training and recovery, make sure your foundation is solid too. A complete multivitamin like the dotFIT Alln1 SuperBlend should be in your daily stack. It supports everything happening under the hood while you're grinding through those early morning WODs.
>
> Keep crushing it! 💪

> Hey [CUSTOMER],
>
> Below is from our Practitioner lectures—also an FAQ and answer that should answer your question.
>
> Have a great evening.
>
> Hi [NAME],
>
> Yes, the gentleman is correct; he can use the AminoFormula (AF) in the ways he describes. But as I mentioned in the lecture, only count AminoFormula as 25–40 g (1–2 scoops) of protein toward your daily goal of 1 g/pound of lean body mass (LBM) of protein, no matter how much you use. This is because you need the traditional food proteins for other health contributions, including satiety. See below FAQ from the lecture and how to use AF as a protein contribution to your diet.
>
> You do not need to pair AminoFormula with NO7—it is optional.
>
> Thanks and have a great day.
>
> Q: There is no protein listed on AminoFormula (AF); can I count it as a protein?
>
> A: You may add 25–40 g (1–2 scoops) of protein to your daily protein intake when using AF as directed, but generally no more, regardless of daily intake. The reason AminoFormula (AF) does not publish a protein amount is because it is purposely not a complete protein, as it contains only the essential amino acids that are directly responsible for protein synthesis activation in proportion to the demand, and therefore serves as a low-calorie, fast-acting, high-anabolic-signaling product for activating muscle protein synthesis (MPS) beyond intact protein itself.
>
> Your total food/shake protein intake throughout the day supplies the remaining amino acids (and they can be made in the body) necessary for the complete and prolonged synthesis. That said, AF can be used to fortify a low-protein meal/food (e.g., plant-based, low-protein breakfast, etc.) to raise the total food protein biological value (BV)—i.e., eliminate the amino acid (AA) limiting factor for MPS indigenous to weaker protein sources. Further, AF is vegan-friendly.
>
> More info:
>
> AF serves to enhance the MPS process beyond protein alone or serve as a low-calorie, high-anabolic extra source of protein. A protein equivalent in 1 scoop of AF is generally counted as approximately 25 g, as it takes approximately 25–35 g of a complete protein to get the amount of the EAA found in one serving of AF, including the 6 g of BCAA. However, regardless of your total AF intake, we advise you only add it as 25–40 g of protein to your 1 g/lb/lean body mass (LBM)/day of protein needs. Mindful that active strength/performance athletes still maintain the 1 g/lb/LBM/day of whole/intact protein sources because different whole-food proteins have other helpful properties/nutrients, including satiety.

>  dotFIT Multivitamin & Mineral for your age and gender (use Over50)
> Take as directed with meals.
>

> Use as directed within daily meal planning (see below) and to make sure you get ~1 gram of protein per pound of lean body mass (or weight if not overweight) daily from all sources, divided 4–5 times daily depending on preference.
>
> Can also be used as a supplement to a meal or by itself (or mixed with desired ingredients) to shore up total daily protein needs.
>

> Use if you do not meet the daily needs of calcium (~1000–1200 mg) from food/shake intake.
>
> Males take 1 only if necessary.

> Take 1–2 daily with a meal if not consuming 8–16 oz/week of fatty fish (take 1 if >8 oz/week but <16 oz; 2 if ≤8 oz); not necessary if consuming ≥16 oz/week unless for athletic recovery purposes, then increase as directed.

>
> AminoFormula (AF)
> Count as 25–40 grams toward total daily protein.
>
> Take 1 scoop ~10 minutes before and may continue to drink during the first 20 minutes of the workout.
>

> Or use both scoops and start ingesting 10 minutes before the workout and consume at a pace so it’s finished at the end of the activity.
>
> Meal Replacement/Substitute Integration
>
> You may also click here for a simple ready-to-go solution, including sample menus with proper calories, foods, and LeanMR meal replacement diet integration.
>
> Weight/Fat Loss Phase:
>
> Meal replacements (MRs) generally supply two small meals a day and allow freedom of choice from traditional foods for the remaining allotted foods/calories. Therefore, use as two small meals within any calorie-restricted meal plan of 4–5 meals, since frequent meals are often better for bodyfat/weight reduction than fewer larger ones, especially as it relates to satiety, preservation of lean body mass (LBM), and energy levels. LeanMR recipes are available here.
>
> Maintenance Phase
>
> Continue to consume 4–5 small meals daily within the caloric allotment that includes 2 MRs for convenience and to help ensure overall diet quality while reducing food costs. A wide variety of healthy recipes, including meal replacement recipes, are available here.

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> Thanks for contacting us. The Sleep Aid contains no ingredients you mention (shellfish or peanuts/tree nuts). The label has warnings (see below) as necessary if you are worried about a rare cross-contamination issue. I also posted a related Q&A below that has more information.

> Sleep Aid label.
>
> Q: Just looking for a clarification on the digestive enzymes. It states it's gluten-free, but when I received it, the bottle stated it's manufactured in a place that may make other products with wheat. So, is this product gluten-free safe for celiac? Usually, I do not take anything labeled this way due to concerns of cross-contamination, unless I reach out to the manufacturer. Please advise. Thank you.
>
> A: The simple answer is to read all labels. While none of our products, except for a few nutrition bars, contain gluten or related ingredients, you only need to read all labels carefully if you believe that any possible/unlikely cross-contamination from facilities that may make products with gluten/wheat may affect your celiac. All our labels have the correct legal warnings when necessary. See below. If you do not see this warning related to your sensitivity, you are good to go. And while the Digestive Enzymes have that warning, and of course it contains no gluten ingredients, by law you must list if the product is made in a facility that may also use wheat for other products—i.e., in case of some unlikely cross-contamination you may fear based on your sensitivity.
>
> “Allergen Warning: This product was produced in a facility that may also process ingredients containing milk, eggs, fish, shellfish, tree nuts, peanuts, wheat, and soybeans.”

**Source answer:**

> Hi [CUSTOMER],
>
> See below for the quick answers on all in red. The Creatine guys have obviously not been on my creatine lectures, where all is explained about loading or not and maintenance levels. And no clue where they got their 0.07 g/lb versus 0.04 g/lb numbers or what they mean ([0.045 is what we use for maintenance based on real athlete usage]). We have never said loading is necessary—it is simply preferred by competitive athletes to achieve an earlier ergogenic effect.
>
> Loading is 0.14 g/lb body weight (I use LBM), and maintenance in conservative science is approximately 3–5 g/day or 0.03 g/kg/day (0.014 g/lb/day), which is too low for powerful active athletes. I only use LBM for loading and maintenance (lower than body weight but more accurate). Our maintenance doses are 5 g/day or 0.045 g/lb LBM/day (larger athletes may need 5–10 g/day*; Ref 2; #7, 8, 10, 46–48). My recommendations are based on my vast, unparalleled experience working with athletes from 450 LBM strongmen all the way to Olympic gymnasts using creatine. And unarguably, no one has more experience than I do with creatine and athletes of all walks.
>
> Below is from our lectures—and, by the way, they (your “creatine experts”) are free to set up a quick call with me any time after they have attended the full lecture. The slides below are from the lecture. I also attached the PDF from one (albeit not the newest). There is also a full creatine episode on SuppBeast.
>
> Call me with any more questions.
>
> Keep up the great work!
>
> Practical Interpretation for ≥200 lb Athletes
>
> Mechanistic and review data support the rationale that larger athletes may need 5–10 g/day to maintain saturation 10.
>
> Large meta-analyses show creatine works across a wide range of maintenance doses, with some performance signals in >5–8 g/day ranges 1620.
>
> No substantive safety concerns emerge at these doses in healthy athletes 351019.
>
> Summary Table
>
> | Question | Takeaway for ≥200 lb athletes | Citations |
> |---|---|---|
> | Is 3–5 g/day “enough” for most? | Yes, for typical-sized users | 3519 |
> | Any rationale for 5–10 g/day in big athletes? | Yes, to sustain saturation in larger bodies | 10 |
> | Any performance advantage of >5–8 g/day? | Some evidence (e.g., jump, leg press) | 620 |
> | Safety at 5–10 g/day long term? | Supported in healthy populations | 351019 |
>
> Figure 2: Summary of evidence on higher creatine maintenance doses.
>
> Bottom line: For a 200+ lb strength/power athlete, 5 g/day is clearly supported, and 5–10 g/day is physiologically reasonable and evidence-compatible when aiming to ensure saturation. Further, empirical data supports higher doses in larger athletes than the one-size-fits-all or low maintenance dose of conservative science.
